# PART 3 — SATURDAY CFB: REASONING PLAYS (in full)

# CFB Reasoning Plays — Saturday, October 31, 2026

**Standing label for everything below: REASONING-LED · CFB-UNCALIBRATED.**
No fitted CFB model exists (governing doc: `CFB_MODEL_DECISION.md`, 2026-10-09 — the NFL engine's sigma, home-field, and calibration may not be copied to college, and no college fit has been built since). Nothing here is a GSE model probability. The ratings cited are **ESPN FPI** (an external model, pulled live 2026-10-31, data updated 2026-10-30) and **raw scoring margins** (unadjusted for opponent or pace). Where a probability is quoted, it is **the market's own** Shin-devigged fair probability (DraftKings via ESPN pickcenter) and is labeled as such. These candidates are **not sized like NFL plays** — they are the reasoning trail Garrett asked for: matchup first, situation, health, venue/weather, market last and small.

**Prices:** DraftKings via ESPN, pulled 2026-10-31 ~05:06 UTC (single book; lines move).
**Slate:** 47 FBS games. Every game was scored. 8 candidates survived; 39 are PASS with reasons.
**Health data disclosure:** ESPN's CFB injury feed lists exactly 3 players FBS-wide today (Florida WR Kahleil Jackson — Out; two "Active" designations). Treat player availability as **unknown**, not clean, for every game below. No candidate's case rests on injury information.

**Method in one paragraph:** For each game: (1) FPI-implied spread proxy = FPI gap + 2.5 home field (0 at neutral sites) vs the market spread → "FPI Δ"; (2) raw scoring-margin-per-game gap (from ESPN standings PF/PA) vs the market spread → "margin Δ"; (3) unit matchups from FPI efficiencies (offense/defense efficiency, 0–100 scale); (4) form/streaks, rest days and last/next opponents from team schedules; (5) game-time weather from NWS for outdoor finalists. A candidate needed both deltas pointing the same way with real size, plus a matchup or situational story — then the market price was checked last. Where FPI (schedule-adjusted) and raw margin disagreed, FPI won the argument and the game was passed. That rule killed several tempting raw-margin games (Missouri, Oregon State, Utah — see PASS list).

---

## TIER 1

### 1. Kansas State +3 (−112) at Colorado — 2:00 PM ET, Folsom Field, Boulder
**The strongest candidate on the slate.**
- **Market:** Colorado −3 (ML −155). The market's own devigged fair ML: Colorado 58.7% / Kansas State 41.3%.
- **Weighed:** FPI rates Kansas State **+5.7** vs Colorado **−2.0** — a 7.6-point neutral-field gap, i.e. FPI makes K-State roughly a 5-point favorite *in Boulder*; the market makes them a 3-point dog (FPI Δ −8.1, the largest adjusted-rating disagreement on the board). Raw margin agrees violently: K-State +11.7 pts/game vs Colorado −10.0 (margin Δ −24.7). Colorado's offense is the weakest unit in the game (efficiency 39.9) against a competent K-State defense (57.5). Form: Colorado has lost 5 straight, including 7–29 vs Texas Tech and 24–57 at Oklahoma State last week; its only competitive recent outing was 24–27 vs Utah.
- **Discounted:** K-State's raw margin is partly built on September blowouts of weak teams — its last four are 1–3 (losses at Cincinnati, vs Houston, at Arizona State; win over Kansas). That is why the FPI Δ, not the −24.7 margin Δ, carries the case. Colorado's defense (66.0 efficiency) is genuinely respectable, and Folsom Field sits at ~5,345 ft — a real visitor tax the 2.5-point generic home-field in the proxy may understate.
- **Weather:** 57°F, wind 3 mph, 15% precip — no factor.
- **Missing:** Any verified injury/availability information for either team (feed is effectively empty).
- **Why it survives:** Getting points with the team both the adjusted rating and the raw performance record say is better — against an opponent in a five-game freefall whose offense cannot score. The spread (+3) is the vehicle; the ML (+130) exists but the market's own fair ML (41.3%) makes the points the cleaner expression.

### 2. Ohio State −6.5 (−112) at USC — 7:30 PM ET, LA Memorial Coliseum
- **Market:** Ohio State −6.5 (ML −245; market fair ML: OSU 68.8%).
- **Weighed:** FPI Δ −6.5 and margin Δ −11.6 — both signals say the market is 6–12 points short on Ohio State (FPI proxy: OSU by ~13 on the road). The unit story is the cleanest on the slate: Ohio State's offense (efficiency 83.1, #4) against USC's defense (45.2) is a mismatch; Ohio State's defense (86.8, #7) is the best unit in the game, against even USC's elite offense (80.3). Ohio State is 6–1 (AP #2), on a W5 streak, and **rested** — last played Oct 17 (beat Indiana 19–15) while USC played Oct 24 and lost 7–24 to Wisconsin. Rest edge: Ohio State.
- **Discounted:** Cross-country trip; the Coliseum is a real home field; USC's offense can score on anyone in a one-game sample. **Weather:** rain showers likely at kickoff (57% precip, 68°F, wind 5 mph) — wet conditions add variance and compress favorite covers; this is priced into the tier, not ignored.
- **Missing:** Injury/availability data for both teams (unknown, as everywhere today).
- **Why it survives:** Two independent signals agree the number is short, the defensive mismatch is structural (not form-based), and the favorite is the rested team. The rain is the reason this is not ranked #1.

### 3. Nebraska −7.5 (−110) vs Washington — 8:00 PM ET, Memorial Stadium, Lincoln
- **Market:** Nebraska −7.5 (ML −298; market fair ML: Nebraska 72.7%).
- **Weighed:** The twin-aligned game: FPI Δ +7.2 and margin Δ +7.5 — two unrelated signals land on the same ~7-point disagreement with the market (FPI proxy: Nebraska by ~14.7 at home). Nebraska's defense (efficiency 81.8) vs Washington's offense (47.6) is the core mismatch; Nebraska's scoring margin is +20.1/game vs Washington's +5.1. The market's caution is traceable: Nebraska has lost two straight. **The context erases most of that discount** — the losses were 17–20 to Indiana (#4) and 14–17 at Oregon, two one-score games against top teams. Washington's last four: losses to Minnesota, at USC, and 24–41 at home to Iowa, with one win at Purdue.
- **Discounted:** Nebraska's offense (70.3) is good-not-great, so a cover needs the defense to hold Washington near its recent outputs; Washington has played the tougher recent schedule, so its margin is somewhat deflated.
- **Weather:** 63°F, wind 7 mph, 20% precip — no factor.
- **Missing:** Injury/availability data (unknown).
- **Why it survives:** When the adjusted rating and the raw record independently produce the same number, and the favorite's losing streak dissolves under opponent context, the market is pricing a narrative the data doesn't support.

---

## TIER 2

### 4. Illinois −2.5 (−112) at Maryland — 12:00 PM ET, SECU Stadium, College Park
- **Market:** Illinois −2.5 (ML −135; market fair ML: Illinois 55.4%).
- **Weighed:** FPI Δ −5.3, margin Δ −7.5 — signals make Illinois a ~8–10 point team here; the market asks them to cover 2.5. Matchup: Illinois' offense (efficiency 76.5) against Maryland's defense (57.1); Maryland's offense (38.3) is bottom-tier. Form: Illinois W2; **Maryland has lost 5 straight** and is −5.0 pts/game on the season at 2–5.
- **Discounted:** Illinois' own defense is poor (40.1), which is how a short road favorite fails; Maryland is home and desperate, the classic dead-cat spot.
- **Weather:** 60°F, wind 6 mph, 0% precip — clean.
- **Missing:** Injury data (unknown).
- **Why it survives:** Short number, both signals clear it by 2–3×, and the opponent's offense is the weakest unit on the field.

### 5. New Mexico −14.5 (−115) at San José State — 4:00 PM ET, CEFCU Stadium, San Jose
- **Market:** New Mexico −14.5 (ML −650; market fair ML: New Mexico 84.6%).
- **Weighed:** Margin Δ −13.3 (New Mexico +25.7 pts/game, 6–1) and FPI Δ −4.0 both point the same way. The unit mismatch is extreme: San José State's offense (efficiency **20.7** — near the bottom of FBS) against New Mexico's defense (**81.9**). SJSU is 4–4 with a −2.1 margin and lost 13–28 to Nevada last week; New Mexico beat North Dakota State 21–16 last week — the same NDSU that is a 24.5-point favorite over UTEP today, a useful cross-check on the Lobos' level.
- **Discounted:** Laying 14.5 on the road is always a tax; FPI's schedule adjustment shrinks the gap hard (FPI Δ is the smaller voice here — New Mexico's margin was built partly on a soft schedule). New Mexico's offense is only middling (50.6), so the cover likely needs the defense to keep SJSU near 10.
- **Weather:** 63°F, wind 7 mph, 3% precip — clean.
- **Missing:** Injury data (unknown).
- **Why it survives:** The defensive mismatch is the single most lopsided unit pairing on the slate, and both signals still clear the number after the schedule discount.

### 6. Pittsburgh −8.5 (−108) vs Georgia Tech — 7:30 PM ET, Acrisure Stadium, Pittsburgh
- **Market:** Pittsburgh −8.5 (ML −305; market fair ML: Pitt 73.2%).
- **Weighed:** FPI Δ +5.1, margin Δ +5.8 — signals cluster around Pitt by ~14. Matchup: Pitt's offense (58.3) against Georgia Tech's defense (**34.8**, the weakest unit in the game); Pitt's defense (64.6) also wins its side against Tech's offense (54.5). Georgia Tech (2–5) gave up 49 to Boston College last week and has lost 3 of its last 4.
- **Discounted:** Pitt has lost 3 straight — stated plainly. Context: two were one-score games (UNC 17–21, at BC 21–27) and the third was at #1 Miami (23–52). The streak is schedule-shaped, not collapse-shaped — but a home favorite on an L3 still carries real "something is wrong" risk that the ratings can't see (e.g., a QB situation the empty injury feed would hide).
- **Weather:** 57°F, wind 3 mph, 2% precip — clean.
- **Missing:** Injury data (unknown) — flagged hardest here, because this is the candidate where an unreported QB absence would most change the read.
- **Why it survives:** Both deltas clear 5 with the game's worst unit (Tech's defense) facing Pitt's offense at home; the L3 is explained by opponent quality, in the open.

### 7. South Florida −7.5 (−112) vs UAB — 12:00 PM ET, Raymond James Stadium, Tampa
- **Market:** South Florida −7.5 (ML −305); total 41.5 — the lowest total among candidate games.
- **Weighed:** FPI Δ +6.5, margin Δ +5.2 — FPI makes USF (FPI −2.1) roughly a 14-point home favorite over UAB (−13.6); USF's scoring margin is +11.4/game vs UAB's −1.3. UAB's offense (efficiency 24.6) against USF's defense (54.6) is a severe mismatch.
- **Discounted:** The 41.5 total is the market saying this is a rock fight — and USF's own offense is weak (40.5), so covering −7.5 requires manufacturing ~24 points in a game the market expects to produce ~41 total. The signal is real; the *cover path* is narrower than the deltas suggest. That is why this is Tier 2 and not Tier 1.
- **Weather:** 82°F, wind 13 mph E, 4% precip — warm, mild wind; no material factor.
- **Missing:** Injury data (unknown).
- **Why it survives:** Both deltas clear 5 and the opponent's offense is bottom-five in FBS by efficiency; the low total is disclosed as the counterweight rather than waved away.

## TIER 3 — highest variance

### 8. Southern Miss −3.5 (−102) at UL Monroe — 3:30 PM ET, Malone Stadium, Monroe
- **Market:** Southern Miss −3.5 at −102, the cheapest price on this list (ML −166).
- **Weighed:** FPI Δ −5.8, margin Δ −6.9. UL Monroe profiles as the weakest team on today's slate: FPI −25.0, scoring margin −17.1/game, offense efficiency **18.9** and defense **23.4** — every unit edge in the game belongs to Southern Miss (FPI −13.2, margin −6.7), including Southern Miss' mediocre offense (29.1) against ULM's defense (23.4).
- **Discounted:** Both teams are bad (2–5 at 1–6), and bad-team games carry the widest outcome distributions in the sport — the rating gap is real but the floor on execution is low on both sides. This is a "least-bad team at a short price" case, nothing more.
- **Weather:** 75°F, wind 5 mph, 21% precip — no factor.
- **Missing:** Injury data (unknown).
- **Why it survives:** A short, cheap number where every measurable unit favors one side; sized and tiered as the variance play it is.

- **Minnesota at Indiana −20.5:** Δ +5.2 / −5.3 — a dead-even signal conflict at a huge spread. PASS.
- **Arizona State at BYU −16.5:** Δ +1.8 / −2.9 — conflict inside noise at a huge spread. PASS.
- **Northwestern at Oregon −3.5:** Δ +2.2 / −9.2 — raw margin loves Northwestern, the adjusted rating doesn't; FPI wins ties here. PASS.
- **Kansas at TCU −6.5:** Δ −0.9 / −3.9 — sub-threshold. PASS.
- **Colorado State at Utah State −6.5:** Δ +0.1 / −9.2 — direct conflict. PASS.

- **FIU at Liberty −14:** Δ +2.1 / −0.3 — no signal at −14. PASS.
- **Utah −7 at Cincinnati:** Δ −1.0 / −6.9 — FPI says −7 is correct; the margin dissent is unadjusted, and Utah has the BYU rivalry game next week (a live lookahead discount). PASS.
- **Virginia at Wake Forest −12.5:** Δ −1.1 / −3.1 — the market is already past the adjusted rating on Wake. PASS.
- **Nevada at UCLA −22.5:** Δ −0.6 / −7.6 — FPI endorses the huge number; margin-only dissent. PASS.
- **South Carolina at Oklahoma −3.5:** Δ +0.7 / −10.5 — dead conflict between the two signals. PASS.
- **Purdue at Penn State −10:** Δ +2.2 / +0.9 — inside noise. PASS.
- **App State at Georgia Southern −5.5:** Δ −0.2 / −11.1 — FPI silent; a raw-margin-only case doesn't ship. PASS.
- **Oklahoma State −9.5 at Iowa State:** Δ +2.4 / −5.1 — direct conflict. PASS.
- **Clemson at Florida State −6:** Δ +0.6 / −2.4 — no signal. PASS.
- **Washington State at San Diego State −2.5:** Δ −0.8 / −3.4 — sub-threshold. PASS.
- **Miami −14 at North Carolina:** Δ −3.6 / −16.1 — near-miss: the case leans on raw margin against the #1 team in the country; the adjusted delta (3.6) is too thin to lay 14 on the road, and Miami has Notre Dame on deck. PASS.
- **Missouri −10 at Arkansas:** Δ +2.1 / −7.3 — the market is already past the adjusted rating (FPI proxy: Missouri by ~8); the raw-margin dissent (+11.6/game, built on an elite 79.9 defensive efficiency) is unadjusted, and both teams are off a bye, so no rest edge exists to break the tie. PASS.

## What would change this list

- A fitted CFB rating (the build order in `CFB_MODEL_DECISION.md`: closing-line join → fit sigma/home-field on college residuals → sealed holdout) — until then every number here is borrowed (FPI) or raw (margin).
- A real availability feed: ESPN's CFB injury endpoint returning 3 players FBS-wide is a coverage failure, not a clean bill.
- Closing-line comparison: prices above are ~05:06 UTC snapshots from one book; any candidate whose line moves through the signal (e.g., Kansas State to −1, Ohio State to −9.5) has its case spent and should be re-read before use.

## 4.2 Captured episode intel (transcript-based, captured live Nov 1)

### Thursday batch — captured live Nov 1, 2026 (sign-in via text code; transcripts read in full)

Source note from the capture: SiriusXM transcripts contain frequent automated-transcription errors in names, teams and numbers. Claims below are paraphrased, not quoted; where a name was garbled, context and cross-show reporting were used. Ambiguous items are flagged. All claims are the shows' hosts', not verified facts.

#### 1. FANTASY ALARM — "Kordell Stewart, Former NFL QB" — Oct 29, 1h 37m
Hosts: Howard Bender and Andrew Cooper, with Matt Kelly, Josh Wagner and Shan Blunt; Kordell Stewart joined for a long interview about his new HBO/HBO Max documentary "Slash and Burn" and his dual-threat legacy, with little Week 8 fantasy content in that segment.
- **(a) Injury/availability:** Lamar Jackson practiced in full — encouraging, wheels up for Ravens-Bills. Saquon Barkley was not spotted at Thursday practice; he hurt his hamstring and back in the previous game, and Cooper said he could not be trusted even if he plays. A Seattle backfield player described as Derian Price could return in Week 8, but Bender said he would not start him even if he plays because the workload was unknown; Emmanuel Wilson was still playable and Charbonnet was not back yet. Tajay Spears did not practice, which Bender said could put the ball in Tony Pollard's hands full-time again. Rashee Rice was spotted in practice. A.J. Brown did not practice, concerning given his history. TreVeyon Henderson practiced one day, then did not practice the next and was seen in street clothes — described as bad news on a Thursday. Carolina was missing two offensive linemen in the Tampa Bay loss, and Taylor Moton was later discussed as out for the season.
- **(b) Usage/target share:** In Cooper's Target Report, Savion Williams had taken over the Jaden Reed slot role in Green Bay, had played only two weeks, was getting 3–4 targets per week plus two carries in each of the last two weeks — 5–6 looks combined — and was clearly the Packers' WR3 for DFS and if Matthew Golden's injury worsened. Start Matthew Golden, Christian Watson and Tucker Kraft in Green Bay. The risers named: Omar Cooper Jr. kept his slot role in three-WR sets and stayed outside in two-WR sets when Adonai Mitchell returned, with Mitchell becoming only a three-WR field-stretcher. Casey Concepcion was trending up after showing intermediate-route and yards-after-catch ability rather than only gadget work. Fallers: Sam LaPorta had one catch in the past week, with a slot receiver on a 160-target pace, Jahmyr Gibbs needing 90–100 targets and Jameson Williams able to be a high-end option — too much inconsistency for a high-end tight end. Isaiah Likely had his worst game yet with a quarterback change. Michael Wilson had quietly been worse without Marvin Harrison Jr. Elijah Higgins was a DFS dart-throw only, not a season-long start, at about $2,700, with Arizona expected to use more two-tight-end sets against Dallas. Ja'Marr Chase was on the worst target pace of his career at 7.5 targets per game, with fewer targets than Tee Higgins; Cooper said he was not selling and would rather buy.
- **(c) Sunday takes:** Fade-the-public segment — Patriots -7 at Miami, with 73% of the public on New England: both hosts faded the public, taking Miami plus the points for a possible backdoor cover, while still expecting New England to win. Falcons +2.5 at Tampa Bay, 68% on Atlanta: fade the public, back Tampa Bay, with Baker Mayfield back, Emeka Egbuka expected back, Chris Godwin, Cade Otten and Bucky Irving running behind a solid line. Browns +3.5 at Pittsburgh, 66% on Cleveland: Cooper backed Cleveland to cover and possibly win outright, citing Deshaun Watson's five comeback wins and third/fourth-down conversions; Bender rooted for Pittsburgh and noted Cleveland had not won a regular-season game in Pittsburgh since 2003. Thursday night: Packers -3.5, total 46.5–47. Bender took Carolina plus the points and the over, citing Carolina 13–0 against the spread after a loss. Cooper took Green Bay to cover and the under, citing Carolina losing its starting right tackle. Shan Blunt took Carolina and the over. Josh Wagner took Carolina plus 3.5 and the under, citing the public side in night games at 5–16 against the spread, Green Bay 0–4 against the spread with referee Scott Novak, Jordan Love 9–1 to the first-half under in his last 10 home games, and Carolina 7–1 to the under in primetime since last season. Their parlay, at +950, was Savion Williams over 23.5 receiving yards, Tommy Tremble's longest reception under 10.5 yards, Chuba Hubbard anytime touchdown, and Josh Jacobs over 59.5 rushing yards. For "Hot to Go," Cooper picked Zay Flowers as a possible WR1 of the week against Buffalo's bend-don't-break defense that allows catches and yards after catch; Bender picked Josh Allen, also hedging his Ravens +3 "Dog of the Week" pick; Wagner picked Chase Brown against Tennessee, the matchup in which Quinshon Judkins had popped; Blunt picked Kenneth Walker to "dog walk" Denver.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Strong disagreement on Thursday night, as above. Cooper was the most bearish on Saquon Barkley, saying the Eagles were falling apart, Barkley was banged up, and managers needed to move past preconceived notions about him; he was likewise bearish on Jonathan Taylor after Indianapolis traded away Quentin Nelson and other linemen, saying Taylor owners should be rooting for a trade. Both hosts liked the Jeffrey Simmons trade to Chicago — two first-round picks and a fourth — as a go-for-broke move, arguing draft picks are overrated, with a hit rate of about 50% even on high-end picks, and that Simmons immediately upgraded a defense facing Seattle. Bender was more confident in Carolina's bounce-back and in Chuba Hubbard as a DFS captain; Cooper preferred Jordan Love as captain and a Packers-heavy DFS build, while Wagner preferred Jacobs as captain after his fresh legs and last week's return.

#### 2. THE JEFF RATCLIFFE SHOW — "Trade Deadline Chaos & TNF Preview" — Oct 29, 1h 37m
Host: Jeff Ratcliffe.
- **(a) Injury/availability:** Jalen Coker practiced fully in back-to-back practices and was good to go. Matthew Golden was officially questionable after DNP-DNP-limited; Ratcliffe called it a true 50/50, with information available 90 minutes before kickoff. Saquon Barkley's hamstring was listed first, with a back issue after hurting it against Dallas; Ratcliffe said it did not appear it would keep him out and looked more like a planned practice pattern, and that Barkley would still be an RB2 even with limited work. Damien Pierce went to injured reserve. Tank Bigsby, out with a core-muscle injury requiring four missed games, was about two weeks from returning, around Week 10, and was available in 94% of ESPN leagues — not a regular-league pickup yet, only a very deep-league stash. Lamar Jackson practiced in full and was expected to play; Ratcliffe ranked him QB4. Davante Adams had successful hamstring surgery and was expected toward the short end of a 6–10 week timetable, earliest Week 13 and more likely Week 14 on a Thursday night. Terrance Ferguson's ankle injury kept his return window closed, with at least a full week, possibly two weeks, of practice needed after it opened; Colby Parkinson was a little dinged up and the Rams' tight-end picture was described as unclear. Detroit's Amon-Ra St. Brown missed the first practice with an ankle issue but Dan Campbell said he should be good to go; Jahmyr Gibbs missed with a shoulder issue after going down gingerly, but Ratcliffe's concern was not high. A.J. Brown was limited with his ankle. Tyler Warren was limited with a groin issue and Ratcliffe said it would be "utterly idiotic" for Indianapolis to trade him. Aaron Jones opened the week DNP, described as day-to-day after playing through an MCL sprain that he aggravated; if he missed three straight practices he would be out, otherwise plan for him to play. D.J. Moore returned to limited practice after the bye. Rashee Rice returned to limited practice after an extended hamstring absence, but with no guarantee he would play. Breece Hall started the week with limited work after a DNP start the prior week and was trending in the right direction. Ladd McConkey was in concussion protocol but practiced in full and should be back. Jalen Waddle did not practice with a chest issue; another DNP would mean preparing to be without him. Carnell Tate did not practice with a shoulder issue. Arizona's Tyler Algier had a toe issue and did not practice; Jeremiah Love returned to limited practice with an ankle issue. Jalen Noel went to injured reserve with a toe injury and, per Houston Chronicle reporting cited by Ratcliffe, was expected to have surgery and miss the rest of the season, making him droppable despite a technical Week 13 eligibility date.
- **(b) Usage/target share:** Ratcliffe said he wanted neither Jaden Blue nor Elijah Mitchell for Philadelphia. Blue had only 39 career touches — 38 carries at 3.4 yards per carry and one catch — and Mitchell had not played in a couple of years. He ranked Aaron Jones RB34 and Jordan Mason RB24 on volume, with Jones having the higher ceiling when healthy. He ranked A.J. Brown as a WR2 despite only six catches since returning — 3 for 27, then 3 for 48 — with 16 targets since returning, no catches on three contested targets, but a 24.5 average depth of target in the most recent game. He ranked D.J. Moore WR16 as if returning, Rashee Rice as if not playing until a full practice changed that, Breece Hall RB18 in a split and Braylin Allen as a fringe RB1 if Hall was out, because Allen had no one taking red-zone work from him. For the Chargers he had Justin Herbert QB26, Marion Hampton RB31, Ladd McConkey WR29 and Quentin Johnston WR55. For Arizona he had Jeremiah Love at RB26, with Bam Knight only a reluctant option.
- **(c) Sunday takes:** Ratcliffe's central process point was not to make Thursday decisions that required three days of practice reports, and not to chase early-week rankings. He would not overthink every-week starters: Caleb Williams over Jacoby Brissett, and Patrick Mahomes, ranked QB14, still started every week. He would not pick up Mitchell or Blue, would not trade a running back away in a two-for-one, and would not move on from Amari Hampton until the offense showed life — then sell after a big game, not before. He liked Bryce Young as a streamer against Green Bay but would not tinker into him otherwise, and he did not regard Bo Nix as streamable after most of his prior-week production came from one long touchdown run worth 12.2 points and one long run-after-catch touchdown. For Thursday night, Golden's status did not move Christian Watson — still top 10 — or Tucker Kraft — already TE3 — or Josh Jacobs — top 10 — and Jordan Love, QB8, might tick only to QB9. Chuba Hubbard was still top 10 on volume; Tetairoa McMillan and Jalen Coker were both top-15 options; there was no playable Carolina tight end, with Darren Waller too deep. Prop leans he discussed, for fun rather than as locks: no on Jordan Love passing yards at 262.5 or Bryce Young at 252.5; a sneaky Tucker Kraft receiving line at 43.5 on FanDuel versus 47.5 on DraftKings if Golden was out; Chuba Hubbard receiving yards at 15.5 on BetMGM; no on Christian Watson 71.5, Coker 62.5, McMillan 71.5 or Golden 57.5 receiving yards; and a fun Mitchell Evans anytime-touchdown price at 9-to-1, with Evans also discussed at over 6.5 yards. For DFS showdown, he leaned to the quarterbacks as chalky captains, Watson as the most interesting captain, Hubbard as the cheapest core piece, Savion Williams at $4,400 as a necessary salary saver, and said it was not a good slate for super-savers, with Tommy Tremble at $2,600 and Mitchell Evans at about $1,600.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Ratcliffe repeatedly stressed low confidence in the Philadelphia offense as a whole, no confidence in Mitchell/Blue, and that "not everything is a thing" just because a player is active. He was confident in Lamar Jackson playing if full practice held, in A.J. Brown's underlying usage despite poor results, and in waiting for information rather than guessing. He disagreed with listeners who wanted an immediate start/sit answer before practice reports were complete, and with the idea of dropping Devonta Adams for Mitchell. On trades, he said Josh Downs had requested a trade and could land with Kansas City, Pittsburgh, Seattle or San Francisco, but fantasy managers should only hold until a landing spot was known; Jonathan Taylor had not requested a trade and was unlikely to be moved; Garrett Wilson had not requested a trade.

#### 3. FANTASY FOOTBALL MORNING PRESENTED BY FANTASY POINTS — "Carolina Panthers at Green Bay Packers Thursday Night Football Preview with Injury Updates and Best Bets" — Oct 29, 1h 37m
Hosts: Paul Kelly and Tom Brawley, with John Hansen ("the Guru") for the Thursday-night special.
- **(a) Injury/availability:** Carolina's offensive line was a theme: Damien Lewis out, tackle problems, and Taylor Moton out for the season. Green Bay's Matthew Golden was banged up with a shoulder injury, sat out practices, then was upgraded in practice and was expected to try to play, but was laboring through it the prior week. An edge defender identified as Jalen Phillips was not expected to play, leaving Jordan Love more comfortable in the pocket. Philadelphia: Saquon Barkley was a non-participant in Wednesday's walkthrough with a hamstring problem and an aggravated back, and the hosts would not expect him to play against Washington; Devonta Smith's hamstring had kept him out three of the last four games and he could be held out; Damien Pierce went to IR, leaving Elijah Mitchell as Philadelphia's sixth running back of the season. Denver's Jalen Waddle was a non-participant Wednesday with a chest injury, though Denver remained hopeful he could play against Kansas City. Indianapolis: Josh Downs had reportedly requested a trade, while Tyler Warren returned to limited practice after missing two weeks. Rams: Davante Adams had successful surgery on his left hamstring, with hope for the early side of 6–10 weeks — at best a fantasy-playoff return around Week 13–14 — and Terrance Ferguson's IR window would not be opened that week, leaving Tyler Higby, Colby Parkinson and Davis Allen. Tampa Bay's Emeka Egbuka was in concussion protocol and also listed with a foot injury, but practiced in a limited fashion. Arizona's Michael Wilson was limited in a non-contact jersey with sore ribs suffered against Denver. Buffalo's D.J. Moore was limited in a non-contact jersey with an AC joint sprain after missing time and coming off the Week 7 bye, still iffy; Dalton Kincaid was limited with a chest injury. Chargers' Ladd McConkey was a full participant Wednesday in concussion protocol, with the foot injury no longer listed — a positive. Houston's Jalen Noel went to IR with a toe injury requiring surgery and was out for the year.
- **(b) Usage/target share:** Bryce Young had thrown 35 or more passes in seven straight games before last week, and averaged 0.51 fantasy points per dropback against two-high safety looks, which Green Bay plays often. Carolina's passing game was described as condensed around Tetairoa McMillan and Jalen Coker. Green Bay gave up the third-most receiving yards per game and second-most receptions per game to outside receivers, but the third-fewest yards per game to slot receivers — better for McMillan than Coker, though Coker also plays outside. Green Bay's run game was dead last in the NFL in rushing attempts, rushing yards and yards per carry at 2.9 yards per carry, more than a full yard behind league average. Josh Jacobs had 11 carries for 36 yards in his return, but 5 catches for 55 yards and a touchdown, with seven targets and a second touchdown that glanced off his hands. Before a knee injury last year he averaged 3.1 receptions and 26.3 receiving yards per game; after it, 1.3 receptions and under 7 receiving yards per game over the final seven games. Carolina allowed the most rushing yards per game to running backs, almost 127 per game. Chuba Hubbard had been a top-10 fantasy back through seven weeks and owned the Carolina backfield, with Trevor Etienne as an unclear No. 2. Christian Watson against cover three — which Carolina played 48% of the time, third-highest in the NFL — averaged 2.93 yards per route run with a 26% target share; Hansen said Watson averaged 140% more fantasy points per route run against cover three than Golden, whose yards per attempt against cover three was 6.36. McMillan against two-high safeties averaged 2.71 yards per route run, up 37.5% versus single-high, and lines up at left wide receiver 44% of the time, where Green Bay allowed the most schedule-adjusted fantasy points to outside receivers.
- **(c) Sunday takes:** Game of the week, Kelly: Baltimore at Buffalo, total 54.5, Buffalo -3, with Lamar Jackson's return especially important for Derrick Henry, whose with/without-Lamar splits were described as stark, and for Zay Flowers. Brawley's game of the week: Chicago at Seattle as a measuring-stick game, with Seattle's backfield possibly getting Darien Price and Zach Charbonnet back and eventually splitting with Kenneth Walker, and Luther Burden's route share possibly rising after Cole Kmet's knee injury. Sneaky shootouts: Kelly picked Raiders at Jets, total 45.5, Jets -1.5, with Garrett Wilson as a squeaky-wheel candidate and a possible 60/40 Breece Hall/Braylin Allen split; Brawley picked Minnesota at Detroit, total 46–46.5, Lions -3 to -3.5, noting Jamir Gibbs had failed to reach 10 PPR points in each of his two games against Minnesota last year. Games to avoid: Kansas City at Denver, total 40.5, and Philadelphia at Washington on Sunday night, total 40.5, Washington -6, with Terry McLaurin out for the season and Washington's backfield weakened by Jacory Croskey-Merritt's IR stint. Best bets from Brawley, who was cited at nearly a 64% player-prop win rate: Kenneth Walker over 82.5 rushing yards against Denver — bet up to 86.5/87.5 — after 173 yards on 23 carries in the season opener against Denver, 128-plus yards in four of six games, a league-best 5.84 yards per carry and an 18% explosive-run rate; Amon-Ra St. Brown over 74.5 receiving yards against Minnesota — bet up to 77.5 — because Minnesota blitzes about 74% of the time and St. Brown was targeted on 38% of his routes against the blitz at 2.84 yards per route run; Zay Flowers over 76.5 receiving yards against Buffalo — bet up to 81.5 — with 3.82 yards per route run overall, second in the league, a 32% target rate per route, 4.53 yards per route run against cover three/four, and 5.60 yards per route run from the slot, where he ran 57% of his routes; and Josh Jacobs over 14.5 receiving yards Thursday night — bet up to 17.5. Hansen's Thursday-night special was Tetairoa McMillan over receiving yards, projected at roughly 250 passing yards for Bryce Young and about 90 yards plus a touchdown for McMillan, and he also liked Jacobs over 14.5 rushing attempts. For Thursday night itself, Kelly and Brawley both leaned to Jordan Love over Jared Goff in a listener question, citing Love's easier matchup and multiple touchdown passes in every game this season, and both said to keep playing Golden, Kraft and Jacobs if Golden was active, with no snap-count news.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** The hosts were confident in the three prop bets above, in McMillan as the Thursday-night special, and in Jordan Love as a top option that night. They were much less confident in the Chargers — "prove it to me" before starting anyone, with only McConkey even considerable, and then only uncomfortably against a Rams defense that was among the toughest against opposing WR1s — and in Jalen Waddle, described as a thin play even if active, a lineup killer as WR48 in fantasy points per game through seven weeks, and a drop candidate in 10-team leagues. They disagreed with the idea of holding Davante Adams without an IR spot, with Kelly saying it was a real decision and Brawley saying he would try to hold in most scenarios because of touchdown upside and because Adams would be highly coveted on waivers if dropped.

#### 4. ROTOWIRE FANTASY SPORTS TODAY — "Simmons Trade & Week 8 Preview" — Oct 29, 1h 37m
Hosts: Jeff Erickson and Nick Whalen, with guests Will Ty (FantasySpin), Adam Kaufman, Chris Clegg and Ian Hartz.
- **(a) Injury/availability:** Baltimore's Kyle Hamilton missed practice with a knee injury and Roquan Smith also missed, but both were reported back later in the show. Buffalo's D.J. Moore remained in a non-contact jersey with an AC joint sprain and Dalton Kincaid in a non-contact jersey with a chest injury; both bore watching over the next 24 hours. New England's A.J. Brown and TreVeyon Henderson both missed Thursday practice after missing Wednesday — a second straight miss that raised worry, with a bump for Rhamondre Stevenson and a good spot for Hunter Henry against Miami. Philadelphia had no Saquon Barkley, Devonta Smith or Davian Wicks at practice; Damien Pierce was on IR, Elijah Mitchell was on the practice squad but got run Monday night, and Jaden Blue was signed to the active roster. Tank Bigsby was expected to return maybe after Week 9 against the Giants, on a timeline thought to be around six weeks rather than just four games. Tennessee's Tajay Spears was not practicing and Carnell Tate missed a second straight practice with a shoulder injury. Cincinnati's Ja'Marr Chase did not practice with an ankle issue plus rest, which the hosts did not regard as a big deal. Detroit's Amon-Ra St. Brown and Jahmyr Gibbs sat Wednesday; Gibbs returned Thursday with a shoulder issue, and St. Brown's Wednesday absence was described as a planned veteran day off, with a full return Thursday expected. Arizona's Tyler Algier had a toe issue and did not practice; Jeremiah Love was limited in returning from an ankle injury that had kept him out since October 9. Houston's Jalen Noel was on IR and expected to miss the rest of the season after toe surgery.
- **(b) Usage/target share:** Jeffrey Simmons to Chicago cost a 2027 first-round pick, a 2028 first-round pick and a 2027 fourth-round pick. Whalen said Simmons ranked No. 1 among defensive tackles since the start of last season in sacks, pressures, tackles for loss, quick pressures and turnovers caused by pressures. Tony Pollard, on an expiring contract and coming off his best game of the year, was the Titans' plausible next sell candidate; Tajay Spears was not viewed as a No. 1 back, Nicholas Singleton had been a healthy scratch at times, and Seth McGowan had 11 carries the prior week as proof of concept. Jahmyr Gibbs averaged almost 28 touches per game and, despite Detroit's bye, still had nine more touches than anyone else in the NFL, with Jonathan Taylor and Derrick Henry tied for second; Detroit had essentially no backup usage, with the next backs at 16 carries on the season and one with zero carries. Jameson Williams was described as touchdown-dependent with waxing and waning target volume, but with four touchdowns in his last two games — a classic best-ball player who frustrates weekly start/sit decisions. Omar Cooper Jr. was cited at 4.7 yards per route run, with only four other players over 3.0, blending yards-after-catch and downfield threat. Trey Tucker averaged 14.3 PPR points per game, essentially matching Justin Jefferson at 14.4. Chase Brown's longest rush of the season was 13 yards, and he had 110 touches without a gain of 15-plus yards, versus 66 for the next player, Kendre Miller; after the bye he played 76% of snaps. Rashad White, in his last game before the bye, played 70% of snaps, handled 54% of designed rush attempts, ran routes at a 55% rate and had a 15% target share for 15 fantasy points; Hartz ranked him RB17 against Philadelphia. Josh Jacobs was Hartz's RB9, with 31 touchdowns in 33 regular-season games for Green Bay, while Marshawn Lloyd's longest run was 12 yards. Colston Loveland was Hartz's TE4 against Seattle, with 65 and 69 yards and a touchdown in each of his last two games with Caleb Williams back, and a team-high 19 targets over those two games. Patrick Mahomes was Hartz's QB19 against Denver, averaging a career-low 12 rushing yards per game, with Xavier Worthy and the rest of the receiving corps unable to separate.
- **(c) Sunday takes:** Bills-Ravens split the hosts: Erickson liked Baltimore in a back-to-the-wall game, Whalen liked Buffalo at home coming off a bye, while noting Buffalo had won its last two games by only a field goal and that 3 versus 3.5 mattered. Patriots at Miami: Kaufman loved New England inside a touchdown, citing Drake Maye at 16–0 straight up and 12–4 against the spread as a favorite of at least a field goal, plus rest advantage after playing the prior Thursday, and a sell-high spot on Miami; Ollie Gordon was not expected to do much against New England's run defense. Rams at Chargers: Kaufman said the Chargers were on a road to 0–9, with no one to trust — Herbert should have been sold, McConkey could not stay on the field, Hampton "absolutely stinks" in his words — and the Rams would win. Eagles at Commanders: Kaufman liked points, total 40–41, Washington -5.5 to -6, with Philadelphia live for a backdoor cover, citing Nick Sirianni 7–1 to the over against Washington over four years and Dan Quinn 8–1 to the over against the NFC East since last season; Jaden Daniels' scrambling against a bottom-10 run defense was a plus, while Philadelphia's backfield was Saquon or Blue only. Raiders at Jets: an over spot at 45-plus, with a slight Jets lean, Geno Smith's revenge game, Braylin Allen start-worthy if Hall was out, and Garrett Wilson, Omar Cooper and Adonai Mitchell all in play. NFC North odds discussed: Vikings still favorites, Bears +280, Lions +290, Packers +1125 to +1200. Minnesota's offense was flagged as historically weak for an unbeaten team — first unbeaten team entering its seventh game averaging under 21 points per game since the 1978 Rams, with only 10 offensive touchdowns, fewest by a 6–0 team since 1934 — and the Lions were liked at home, with Jared Goff described as elite against the blitz. Thursday night DFS: the final FantasySpin lineup was Jordan Love, Tucker Kraft, Chuba Hubbard, Matthew Golden, Tetairoa McMillan and Darren Waller; the hosts said McMillan offered floor, Coker was the boom/bust inflection point, Golden should be avoided if possible on injury grounds, and Bryce Young's prior week was the second-worst EPA-per-dropback game of his career. Hartz's prop of the week was Jalen Warren over 24.5 receiving yards, averaging 42 and clearing that mark in every game this season.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Erickson and Whalen disagreed on Bills-Ravens, and both were skeptical that Minnesota could "keep getting away with it" or that Pittsburgh, described as bringing "a knife to a gunfight" against Josh Allen in the playoffs, was a true contender. They were confident Simmons was worth the price for Chicago because of its quarterback and coaching situation, unlike the Khalil Mack trade era with Mitchell Trubisky and Matt Nagy, and that Simmons would make Chicago's defensive backs look better. They were also confident in Gibbs and St. Brown as the two most dependable fantasy teammates, and in Rashad White over Jonah Coleman, Bam Knight and Braylin Allen in the RB3 tier, while Coleman was the better long-term play. They warned that Tennessee, in clear sell mode after trading its best player three days before a game, would struggle to rally against Cincinnati, whose team total for Tennessee was down to about 16–16.5 points.

#### 5. FANTASY LIFE — "Week 8 Rankings & TNF Preview" — Oct 29, 1h 37m
Hosts: Kendall Valenzuela and Adam Ronas, with former offensive coordinator Scott Turner.
- **(a) Injury/availability:** Philadelphia's backfield was a running joke and a warning: Damien Pierce went to IR after Ronas picked him up, Jalen Noel also went to IR after being picked up, Will Shipley was hurt in the first quarter, Tank Bigsby was expected back maybe after Week 9 against the Giants on a timeline thought to be around six weeks, Elijah Mitchell was the next pickup and Jaden Blue the next after that — with a suggestion to simply avoid the Philadelphia backfield. Saquon Barkley played the prior week with a hamstring injury, then suffered a back injury in the game, looked in pain and labored throughout, going in and out; Ronas said he would be stunned if Barkley played, while Valenzuela said if he was declared going, managers would play him. Jeffrey Simmons was traded to Chicago that morning for two first-round picks, with Tennessee expected to sell more, including possibly Tony Pollard. The Chargers kept Mike McDaniel as offensive coordinator after the bye, with Jim Harbaugh expressing confidence in him; Trey Harris was dealing with a hamstring injury and was cut by Ronas in some leagues. Garrett Wilson said he did not want to be traded but wanted to win; the Jets were 24–41 since drafting him 10th overall in 2022 and 2–5 this season, and Wilson now faced target competition from Omar Cooper Jr., Mason Taylor and Adonai Mitchell, who played about 55% of snaps on his return. Matthew Golden was questionable but the team was optimistic he could play Thursday night, dealing with shoulder and foot injuries and not at 100%, and dropping passes; Savion Williams was a deep flex consideration if Golden was out. Tucker Kraft was Valenzuela's TE4–5 for Thursday night, getting 4–5 catches per game with a touchdown last week. Rams: Davante Adams had successful surgery, with a return hoped for toward the shorter end of 6–10 weeks, but the hosts said not to hold your breath; Tyler Higby was Valenzuela's TE17, with about a 14% target share since Week 3, playing 74% of snaps last week versus 69% each for Colby Parkinson and Davis Allen, who had the prior week's two-touchdown game. A.J. Brown came off a high ankle sprain with a brutal drops game, then a short-week Thursday game; his usage remained strong at a 94% route rate and 46% air-yard share, and the hosts expected improvement against Miami, ranking him WR12–16, but warned that another bad game would raise real worry. Jalen Noel was expected to miss the rest of the season after surgery. Arizona's Jeremiah Love was limited Wednesday, returning from a sprained left ankle suffered in the preseason that had kept him out since October 9 and the past two weeks; Bam Knight had looked better than Tyler Algier as the fill-in. David Montgomery was week-to-week, not on IR, possibly back after the bye, after three touchdowns in Week 1 and then essentially nothing. Terrance Ferguson would not be designated to return from IR that week.
- **(b) Usage/target share:** The Chargers scored a season-low 6 points against Kansas City on 259 total yards, lowering their league-worst scoring average to about 15 points per game; Justin Herbert was only a SuperFlex play, Marion Hampton was touchdown-dependent and not heavily involved in the passing game, and only Ladd McConkey was even considerable, ranked WR28 against a Rams defense allowing the second-fewest fantasy points per target to opposing WR1s. Kyler Murray had run more in his last two games, averaging 45 rushing yards, but averaged only 13.8 fantasy points per game in four healthy games and 30.8 rushing yards per game, with a median projection of 16.8 and a best score of 22.3 against Detroit — a borderline QB1. Caleb Williams was ranked QB11 against Seattle, which had allowed the second-fewest fantasy points to quarterbacks and no quarterback to reach 200 passing yards this season — Drake Maye 178, Brock Purdy 189, Patrick Mahomes 165 for only six fantasy points, the second-fewest in Mahomes' 132 career starts. Bucky Irving averaged almost 19 touches per game against Atlanta. Emeka Egbuka had target shares of 39% and 40% before missing Week 7; if he returned he was a WR2, otherwise Chris Godwin moved to WR3 territory. Washington against Philadelphia: Philadelphia had allowed a league-high 10 different wide receivers to score 14-plus fantasy points, and the second-most fantasy points to the receiver position; Antonio Williams had 4 catches for 64 yards and a touchdown against Philadelphia in Week 1, while Terry McLaurin's replacement role also involved Stefan Diggs, who had 4 for 55 and a touchdown on nine targets in that game. Rashad White was Valenzuela's RB17 against Philadelphia at 70% of snaps, 54% of attempts and a 15% target share for 15 points in his last game. The Rams' backfield was a 50/50 split last week, with Kyren Williams and Blake Corum each scoring a touchdown; Corum played 44% of snaps last week versus 28%, 27% and 15% in prior weeks, while Williams played a season-low 51%. Chicago's Colston Loveland went from a 12% or lower target share in his first three weeks to 22% in three of his last four games. Garrett Wilson's Fantasy Life rankings ranged widely, from WR14 to WR19, settling around WR18, with Omar Cooper Jr. around WR24–25 and Trey Tucker WR26. Casey Concepcion was Valenzuela's WR27 against Pittsburgh after 23.2 fantasy points the prior week.
- **(c) Sunday takes:** Smash spots: Kyler Murray against Detroit, which had allowed 21-plus fantasy points to opposing quarterbacks and about 48 dropbacks per game; Bucky Irving against Atlanta; Emeka Egbuka/Godwin and Washington's receivers against Philadelphia; and Rashad White against Philadelphia. Downgrades: Caleb Williams against Seattle, all Bears except possibly Rome Odunze on the edge, and Colston Loveland to TE5 against Seattle despite still starting him. Start/sit calls: Jordan Love over Caleb Williams for Thursday night, citing Carolina's cornerback injuries and Love's multiple touchdown passes in essentially every game; play Garrett Wilson, but do not like starting both Jets receivers, making Trey Tucker the odd man out among Wilson, Cooper, Parker Washington, Tee Higgins and Tucker — though the hosts then said if Tucker was to be played, Cooper would be the sit, as the two were close; start Jalen Coker over Ladd McConkey and Jalen Waddle, and Casey Concepcion over both; start Breece Hall over Michael Wilson if Hall played against Las Vegas; do not accept a trade of Matthew Golden and Travis Etienne for Breece Hall and Michael Wilson, because Etienne had done nothing before injury and New Orleans' backs had not run successfully with Alvin Kamara still involved; for a flex choice among Blake Corum, Casey Concepcion and Jordan Mason, take Concepcion, then Mason, then Corum; and do not sit De'Andre Swift against Seattle despite the matchup, nor sit Mason against Detroit. Scott Turner, on Bryce Young, said he had faith in a bounce-back Thursday night because Green Bay had not generated much pressure and Young picked defenses apart when kept clean. On Saquon Barkley, Turner said if he was healthy he would play because he was a competitor and Philadelphia was not giving up at 2–5, but he did not look healthy and the smart move was to wait until fully back. On the Chargers' bye, Turner said a bye could help a team reset and find what its players did well, but he expected little against the Rams. On Kansas City, Turner said Seattle was simply a dominant defense and not a reason to panic or mortgage the future for a receiver. On Fernando Mendoza in Las Vegas, Turner said coaches should stick to their long-term plan — like Carson Palmer sitting behind Jon Kitna or Patrick Mahomes behind Alex Smith — and that not seeing Mendoza this season would be a success if the plan was for him to learn behind a veteran. Thursday night itself: both hosts picked Green Bay to win, despite noting Carolina's cited 13–0 record against the spread coming off a loss.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Valenzuela and Ronas disagreed, then partly reversed, on a guillotine-league choice among Jordan Mason, Blake Corum and A.J. Brown — Ronas favored Brown and Mason, Valenzuela leaned to Corum on gut feeling because of his 16, 14 and 17 points over the last three games, before both settled on playing Brown and Mason because Corum's floor was low without a touchdown and his role depended on game script. They were confident in starting Jordan Love, Chuba Hubbard, Jalen Coker, Tetairoa McMillan and Bryce Young as a borderline QB1 Thursday night, and in Green Bay winning, while Turner was the most optimistic voice on Bryce Young's bounce-back. They were least confident in the Chargers, in any Rams tight end, in A.J. Brown until he proved health, and in the Philadelphia backfield at all.

**Cross-show consensus (Thursday):** Lamar Jackson practicing in full and expected back; Saquon Barkley's hamstring/back status as the biggest Thursday-to-Sunday uncertainty, with most hosts leaning to him missing or being limited; Matthew Golden as a true game-time Thursday decision; the Jeffrey Simmons trade to Chicago for two firsts plus a fourth as the defining trade-deadline move; and Green Bay as the Thursday-night favorite, with Carolina plus the points as the most repeated betting lean across shows.

### Friday batch A — captured live Nov 1, 2026 (transcripts read in full)

#### 6. FANTASY ALARM — "Halloween Themed Week 8 DFS!" — Oct 30, 1h 37m
Howard Bender and Andrew Cooper, with Jimmy Scalia and Brian Houk.
- **(a) Injuries/availability:** Carolina RT Taylor Moton hurt — hosts say Carolina needs a trade replacement. Tampa WR Emeka Egbuka — already ruled out this week (rendered "Buca"). Baker Mayfield — hand injury limiting his running; ran once for one yard last week. Denver RB RJ Harvey — already ruled out. New England RB Trayvon Henderson — skipped a media session, in street clothes, then not at practice with an illness; trade rumors noted; Cooper says he is not counting on him. Detroit S Kirby Joseph — definitely out; S Brian Branch is back. Dallas WR George Pickens — questionable. Jets RB Breece Hall — described as expected/trending to play, which would sap Brealin Allen's value. Arizona RB Jeremiah Love — return discussed, not trusted fully. The hosts' "bring back to life" picks were Omarion Hampton (Bender), Chris Godwin (Cooper), Michael Wilson (Scalia) and Saquon Barkley (Houk); listener picks included Brian Thomas Jr., Zach Charbonnet, Ladd McConkey, Jalen Waddle, Chase Brown and Aaron Jones Sr., the last described as now expected to play.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Chris Godwin — 92% route participation last week and a 45% target share, i.e. 5 of only 11 team targets; hosts cite an 85-yard TD called back earlier in the season and a 9-yard TD called back last week. Jets — Garrett Wilson was an every-down outside receiver; Omar Cooper Jr. was alongside him in two-WR sets and moved to the slot in three-WR sets; Adonai Mitchell had 57% route participation. Rams — Blake Corum at about a 40% snap share. Ravens/Bills — Buffalo was said to allow the most fantasy points to RBs in the passing game and the third-most receptions to RBs per game, at six; Justice Hill caught six passes for 78 yards and a TD against Buffalo last year. Jacksonville TE Brenton Strange — last three games cited at eight targets/caught seven, then seven targets/caught five (transcript wording garbled here). Cowboys TE Jake Ferguson — TE22 in points per game when both CeeDee Lamb and Pickens were healthy, versus TE2 in five games when Lamb missed or left early. Ravens WR Jacoby Lane — more routes and targets than Rashad Bateman last week, and more snaps/targets than Bateman over the last two weeks; about $3,400 on DraftKings. DraftKings prices cited: Jacoby Brissett $5,700, Geno Smith $5,200, Derrick Henry $7,200, Jahmyr Gibbs $9,500, Bhayshul Tuten $5,900, Justice Hill $4,200, Jonah Coleman $4,300, Trey McBride $7,400, Brock Bowers $7,600, next TE $5,300, Strange $4,000, Elijah Higgins $2,700. Hunter Henry projected at 31.8% ownership; T.J. Hockenson at about 3.5%.
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Pay up at QB with Josh Allen, even naked, in Buffalo-Baltimore (54.5 total); build a Lamar Jackson lineup with Zay Flowers. Joe Burrow is a must-win play against Tennessee, and Chase Brown may benefit once Cincinnati gets a lead. Dak Prescott against Arizona's poor pass defense, with Brissett as the cheap other side, almost always stacked with McBride. Geno Smith is a $5,200 revenge-game dart against Las Vegas, but Bender warns Aaron Glenn may panic if the Jets fall behind. Avoid Baker Mayfield in DFS because of the hand injury, and avoid Bo Nix this week — low 40.5 total, Denver as home dogs. At RB: Kenneth Walker Jr. is Bender's favorite pay-up; Javonte Williams is the "boring superstar" who keeps scoring; Rhamondre Stevenson is a start against Miami; Tuten is a mid-tier value against a Colts defense missing DeForest Buckner; Hill is a sneaky cheap pass-game play; Coleman is a cheap Denver correlation play against Kansas City, helped by Harvey being out and by J.K. Dobbins' comment that coaches plan to spread work around. At WR: Lamb is a lock/one-off or Dak stack; Flowers is a must-play; Wilson is a squeaky-wheel play after his sideline outburst, and Cooper is a viable standalone; DJ Moore has DFS upside only, not every lineup, because of shoulder/no-show risk. At TE: McBride over Bowers because Dallas' defense is bad, the Jets have been good against TEs, and Brissett may throw 40–50 times; Dalton Kincaid has upside because Baltimore has allowed a 127 passer rating to slot players; Hockenson is a lean with Joseph out despite Branch; Strange is a cash-game value; Ferguson is contingent on Pickens being out; Higgins is a deep $2,700 punt with contingent upside if McBride were hurt — which the hosts explicitly did not wish for.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Bender does not trust Love's return at Dallas but will play him in DFS because Dallas' defense is bad; Cooper calls Love a treat, especially in redraft, and says it would be better if Tyler Allgeier were out. Both call Bo Nix a trick this week and Corum a trick — betting only on a touchdown. Cooper says he will drop Kyler Murray (the Vikings QB in these transcripts) in every league if he does not produce this week, after years as a "truther." Colston Loveland is a treat for Cooper: top-three TE from Week 8 on is a big commitment, but his usage was never bad and Cole Kmet going down leaves Sam Roush in the inline role. Caleb Williams is preferred over Jared Goff in a six-point passing league. In the Drake Maye vs. Lamar Jackson debate, both lean Lamar because of the shootout, despite Maye's easier matchup. Week 8 fades: Gibbs (price vs. Brian Flores), Goff (Cooper ranks Brissett ahead of him against Minnesota), Jayden Daniels ("eerie"), and DJ Moore ("ultimate trick" — may play, then disappear in the second quarter).

#### 7. THE JEFF RATCLIFFE SHOW — "Comprehensive Week Eight Fantasy Football Preview with Game Analysis, Injury Updates, and Betting Strategies" — Oct 30, 1h 37m
Jeff Ratcliffe, with Ani Sridhar on betting.
- **(a) Injuries/availability:** Lamar Jackson — practicing in full, should play; Ratcliffe is confident he will be as close to 100% as possible because last year's return led to compensatory problems. Christian Watson — left Thursday night in an ambulance/hospital trip, returned, finished 8 catches for 113 yards, including the 53-yarder on which he was hurt; Matthew Golden had 13 targets, seven catches for 79 yards and a TD. Bills WR DJ Moore — limited, limited; needs a full practice Friday or he is a true 50/50. Bengals WR Ja'Marr Chase — dinged up but got to full practice, good to go. Titans RB Tony Pollard — DNP for a non-injury rest day; RB Tajay Spears had yet to practice. Titans WR Carnell Tate — DNP, DNP with a shoulder issue, in a mini-breakout. Cardinals RB Jeremiah Love — limited, limited; Tyler Allgeier DNP then limited with a toe issue; Bam Knight also in the mix. Lions RB Jahmyr Gibbs and WR Amon-Ra St. Brown — DNP to start the week, then full; Dan Campbell gave them rest. Vikings RB Aaron Jones — limited yesterday, trending toward playing. Jets RB Breece Hall — limited in back-to-back practices after not suiting up last week; still uncertain. Tampa WR Emeka Egbuka — limited, in concussion protocol, had shed the non-contact jersey; Ratcliffe expected a full practice Friday (this conflicts with Fantasy Alarm and RotoWire, which say ruled out — see the cross-show note at the end of this batch). Colts TE Tyler Warren — limited back-to-back, trending the right way. Broncos RB RJ Harvey — popped up midweek with a knee issue, limited. Chiefs WR Rashee Rice — limited back-to-back, encouraging; TE Travis Kelce's status was garbled as "full limited with an ankle" — ambiguous. Broncos WR Jalen Waddle — DNP to start, then limited, trending toward playing. Patriots WR described as "the Tin Man" — limited then DNP with what was called a knee issue, not an ankle issue; the name is not fully clear in the transcript — ambiguous. Patriots RB Trayvon Henderson — DNP with an illness; Ratcliffe did not see Sunday status in question yet. Eagles QB situation: Tanner McKee under center; WRs DeVonta Smith and Donte(?) Wicks still not practicing — be prepared for them not to go. Washington RB Saquon Barkley — banged up, picking up injuries, but expected to play; RB Jacory Croskey-Merritt on short-term IR. Washington WR Terry McLaurin — season-ending IR. Washington WR Stefon Diggs — dinged up initially, then back at practice. Washington QB Jayden Daniels — elbow issue with a brace on his left elbow, no concern stated. Seahawks RB Zach Charbonnet — full practice, but will he actually return is still a question; RB Jerian Price — limited as a designated return, not yet activated. Bears RB described as Kyle Monangai/Rohan — DNP, now listed as a thumb issue rather than toe; Ratcliffe is not optimistic — name spelling garbled. Colts QB Daniel Jones — ranked 28th of 28 starting QBs this week.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Thursday night — Chuba Hubbard 22 carries for 81 yards (3.7 per carry), one rushing TD, plus seven catches for 38 yards; Josh Jacobs 21 carries for 39 yards, one rushing TD and one receiving TD, six catches for 13 yards. Bryce Young — 41 attempts, 23 completions, 209 yards, one TD to Jalen Coker; Coker two catches for 36 yards, including a 26-yard TD, on five targets; Tetairoa McMillan 11 targets, six catches for 58 yards. Jordan Love — 23 of his 41 attempts went to Watson and Golden; only four targets went outside the big four of Watson, Golden, Tucker Kraft and Jacobs. Buffalo allows 285.8 passing yards per game and the third-most fantasy points to opposing RBs. Bills RB James Cook — 5.4 yards per carry, six TDs, scored in five consecutive games. Cincinnati allows 4.1 yards per carry. Pollard was the first Titan with three rushing TDs since Derrick Henry in 2021. Steelers RB Jalen Warren — 18 rushing attempts last week; Rico Dowdle played about 40% of snaps. Detroit allows 312 passing yards and 2.7 passing TDs per game. Ratcliffe's ranks cited include: Zay Flowers WR5; Burrow behind only Josh Allen; Tee Higgins top 10; Chase Brown top 12; Brissett QB10 but risky; Dallas the No. 6 QB matchup; Kyler Murray QB12, ahead of Goff, who is outside the top 12; Jordan Addison inside the top 40 but not the top 36; Jordan Mason a fringe RB2; Aaron Jones a flex/backend option; Ashton Jeanty RB15; Bucky Irving RB14; Jonathan Taylor RB13, outside the top 12; Trevor Lawrence top six; Parker Washington a backend WR1; Bhayshul Tuten RB14; Blake Corum RB26/fringe RB2; Justin Herbert QB25; Ladd McConkey WR32; Amari Hampton RB31; Jonah Coleman RB28, rising to RB2 territory if Harvey is out; Rashad White RB21/backend RB2; De'Andre Swift a mid-range RB2; Luther Burden WR20, Rome Odunze a fringe WR3; Colston Loveland a top-five TE.
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Lines as stated on the show: Buffalo -3, 54.5 total; Dallas -7, 54.5 total vs. Arizona; Detroit -3 vs. undefeated Minnesota; Jets -1.5, 45.5 total vs. Las Vegas; Pittsburgh -3.5, 39.5 total vs. Cleveland; Cincinnati -12.5, 46.5 total vs. Tennessee; Jacksonville -10, 46.5 total vs. Indianapolis; Rams -6.5, 45.5 total vs. Chargers; Denver a 1.5-point home dog, 40.5 total vs. Kansas City; New England -7, 43.5 total at Miami; Washington -6, 40.5 total vs. Philadelphia; Seattle -3 vs. Chicago. Do not chase last week's fantasy points; do not roster two streamers; do not pick a QB to "offset" an opponent's WRs — start the QB you think scores most, because if the Jets struggle, Wilson/Cooper and Geno all struggle together. Derrick Henry is a prove-it top-five play against Buffalo. Flowers is borderline elite. Dalton Kincaid is a starter with a very wide ceiling/floor range; Mark Andrews is only a backend streamer. Chase Brown must prove it against Tennessee after the Jeffrey Simmons trade. Brissett is a risky QB10 stream; McBride is an all-world TE; Michael Wilson is a top-20 hope despite not becoming the high-volume WR hoped for after the Marvin Harrison Jr. move. Dak, Lamb and Pickens are all top-12 plays; Javonte Williams has no backfield competition; Jake Ferguson is no longer part of "the hits." Goff is a streaming option, not set-and-forget, because he lacks a rushing floor. Minnesota-Detroit is polar opposites: if Murray cannot produce against Detroit's generous QB defense, it is a real problem; Jefferson is a use, Addison is boom/bust, Hockenson is a rock-solid streamer. Raiders-Jets: Kirk Cousins and Geno Smith are back-to-back streamers at QB17/18; Brock Bowers is a start; Jeanty is an RB2 whose passing-game work sustains him; Trey Tucker is a boom/bust WR3; if Hall plays he is an RB2 and Allen drops to flex, but if Hall is out Allen is basically a backend RB1, because Allen takes red-zone work from Hall while nobody takes it from Allen. Steelers: Warren stays top 10, Dowdle is a flex, Aaron Rodgers is SuperFlex only, DK Metcalf is a backend WR2, Jeremy Bernard (name as rendered) is a top-25 candidate. Browns: Deshaun Watson is a backend QB2, not a one-QB play; two Browns WRs are both WR3s with opposite profiles; Quinshon Judkins is a backend RB2, Dylan Sampson is the complement, Harold Fannin is a streamer. Tampa ran for 258 yards on 49 attempts with only 11 Baker Mayfield passes last week; Irving is a top-12 play, Egbuka a backend WR2 if back, Godwin a flex you do not love, Mayfield SuperFlex only; Bijan Robinson is awesome and could see a huge workload if Brian Robinson is still banged up; Drake London is usable, Michael Penix is SuperFlex only against a bottom-six QB matchup, Kyle Pitts is a TD-dependent streamer. Indianapolis is in a fire sale after trading Quenton Nelson and DeForest Buckner; Lawrence is a top-six play, Washington a backend WR1, Jacoby Myers a flex, Travis Hunter not usable but watch his offensive/defensive usage, Strange is a streamer, Tootin is a front-end RB2. Rams: Kyren Williams is locked in as an RB1, Corum is a fringe RB2, Puka Nacua will be crazy busy without Davante Adams, and Rams TEs should be avoided for streaming because of the rotation (Tyler Higbee in theory, Colby Parkinson DNP/DNP, Davis Allen, rookie Max Claire slow-rolled). Chargers: Herbert is only a SuperFlex option, McConkey is a WR3 after full practices in concussion protocol, no other Chargers WR/TE is wanted, Hampton is the best committee shot because of red-zone use. Kansas City-Denver is Ratcliffe's matchup of the week: do not take Bo Nix's 62-yard rushing TD and long catch-and-run TD to Harvey at face value — he is a backend QB2; Coleman led the backfield in snaps but Harvey and Dobbins scored, so Coleman is RB28 unless Harvey is out. New England-Miami: Drake Maye is rolling; Pop Douglas is next up if the "Tin Man" is out; Hunter Henry is TE10 and a top streamer; Rhamondre Stevenson is top 20; Henderson is only a stash; Malik Willis is in the SuperFlex conversation; Miami WR Caleb Douglas had three targets, two catches, 70 yards and two TDs last week but is only a flex; Malik Washington (ranked 35) is slightly ahead of Douglas (45); Ali Gordon is an RB2 on volume and TDs; Jalen Wright got a full practice but only spells Gordon; Greg Dulcich is not a streamer yet. Philadelphia-Washington: Mackay Lemon is WR3 value, Dallas Goedert is fine, the Eagles backfield is a mess and the answer is not Jaden Blue or Elijah Mitchell; Daniels is a top-10 play, Diggs is a fringe WR2, Antonio Williams is a flex over Treylon Burks, Chig Okonkwo is a tempered streamer. Chicago-Seattle: Sam Darnold is streamable, Ratcliffe thinks Chicago might win, Caleb Williams is a top-12 play regardless of matchup, Burden is the better play over Odunze despite Odunze being the every-down WR, Loveland is a top-five TE with Caleb back, Swift is a mid-range RB2, and do not "take the Rohan cheese" on Monangai.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Ratcliffe says he does not care about fantasy points because they mislead — Hubbard and Jacobs are both sell-high candidates after TD-driven Thursday lines despite poor rushing efficiency. He is confident in Lamar if he plays, but only because reporting says Lamar wants to be 100% before returning. He is willing to give Henry, Chase Brown and others the benefit of the doubt in prove-it spots until they prove him wrong. He is not bullish on Rodgers this week, not confident in McConkey, and not confident in Henderson. Betting ticket from Ani Sridhar, endorsed in part by Ratcliffe: Trae Young double-double at -105 (NBA); Zay Flowers over 83.5 receiving yards at -110 (Ratcliffe says he projects Flowers over that line and notes Flowers can do it in a quarter/half); Amon-Ra St. Brown over 7.5 catches at +123 (eight and nine catches on 13 targets in each game vs. Minnesota last season; Ratcliffe projects 11 targets); Samaje Perine over 20.5 rushing yards at -110 (Ratcliffe projects 33.5 yards and likes a ladder, citing Simmons being gone); Michael Wilson over 56.5 yards at -110; Garrett Wilson over 62.5 yards at -110, with Ratcliffe saying Garrett Wilson needs to get going and could be auditioning for a trade.

#### 8. FANTASY FOOTBALL MORNING PRESENTED BY FANTASY POINTS — "Navigating Week Eight Injuries, Backfield Splits, and Key Matchups for Fantasy Football Success" — Oct 30, 1h 37m
Paul Kelly and Joe Dolan, with John Hansen's "Stank Squad."
- **(a) Injuries/availability:** Carolina LT Taylor Moton — out for the season. Packers WR Matthew Golden — questionable coming into Thursday night. Eagles WR DeVonta Smith — bulky hamstring for a number of weeks, no practice yet this week; Eagles RB Saquon Barkley — hamstring plus a related back issue, no practice yet this week; Kelly/Dolan doubt either suits up at Washington (this is earlier/different from RotoWire's later note that Barkley returned to practice — see the cross-show note). Bills WR DJ Moore — a couple of limited practices, non-contact jersey, shoulder issue all season, ranked WR21; if he plays, he is in the lineup, but re-injury risk is high. Seahawks RB Zach Charbonnet — four straight full practices, real chance to play Monday night; RB Jerian Price — limited after being designated to return from IR, also has a chance; Emmanuel Wilson has been soaking up carries as the fallback. Jets RB Breece Hall — another limited session with a quad injury; ranked RB17, a mid-range RB2 assuming he plays, but hosts are squeamish about a first-game-back split possibly near 50/50 with Brealin Allen. Broncos WR Jalen Waddle — chest issue, DNP Wednesday, limited Thursday, trending toward playing vs. Kansas City. Cowboys WR George Pickens — shoulder and hamstring, limited yesterday; Cardinals WR Michael Wilson — rib injury, limited the last couple of days; Cardinals RB Jeremiah Love — ankle, limited the last two days after a couple of weeks off, return question. Patriots injury report — WR A.J. Brown out yesterday and RB Trayvon Henderson out with an illness; hosts say Henderson is a zero/roster-clogger right now and should not be started. Bears RB described as Kyle Monangai — expected to miss another game (name rendered "Kyle Manon guy" in places — flagged). Bears TE Cole Kmet — out for the season. Washington — lead RB on IR (rendered "Bill" in the transcript) and Philadelphia's Barkley not practicing, so both Sunday-night teams may be without their lead RBs. Eagles RB Elijah Mitchell — his three carries last week were his first since 2023; Jaden Blue was signed to the 53-man roster. Also noted: Jeffrey Simmons traded to Chicago, Quenton Nelson traded, DeForest Buckner traded, and a report that the Rams nixed an A.J. Brown trade because of a degenerative knee condition — now Brown's knee has popped up.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Packers — Josh Jacobs 27 touches for only 52 total yards, six catches, two TDs; Jordan Love 30 of 41 for 288 yards, three TDs, one INT, with multiple TDs in every game this year and a season-high three TDs Thursday; targets were condensed to Watson, Golden, Tucker Kraft and Jacobs. Panthers — Chuba Hubbard has topped 20 touches in four of his last five games; Thursday: 81 rushing yards and a TD plus seven catches for 38 yards; he is RB8 in full-PPR points per game. Bryce Young — 209 yards and one TD Thursday, 163 yards and one TD in Week 7; two straight one-TD-pass games. Tetairoa McMillan — 11 targets, six catches, 58 yards Thursday; his Detroit game was 14 catches, 192 yards, two TDs, but that is his only TD game of the season and he has been scoreless in his other six games; last three weeks described as underwhelming. Jalen Coker — two or fewer catches in three of his last four games, but Thursday's TD saved a double-digit PPR day. Brealin Allen — among 44 qualifying RBs: No. 3 in yards after contact per attempt, No. 5 in explosive run rate, No. 9 in missed tackles forced per attempt. Rams backfield in the cited game — Kyren Williams 22 snaps vs. Blake Corum 18 snaps, 9.2 expected fantasy points vs. 6.2, a near-perfect 50/50 series-to-series rotation over the first seven drives; Corum does not run routes. Caller flex projections — Antonio Williams 8.3 PPR points, Corum 9.1, Rico Dowdle 9.5, Caleb Douglas 8.6, all within about one point. Jets WR Omar Cooper Jr. — back-to-back 100-plus-yard games. Geno Smith — just outside the hosts' top-12 QBs. Hunter Henry — TE11 against Miami. Josh Allen since 2023 in games with a total of 50 or higher — 25.2 expected fantasy points and 30.4 actual fantasy points per game; Buffalo allows the third-most fantasy points to opposing QBs; hosts took the over at 53.5, now 54.5. Chase Brown — Samaje Perine vultured a TD last week while Brown was looked at on the sideline; Brown still has the role, and Perine should be rostered in almost every format as a high-end handcuff. Cowboys WR Ryan Flournoy — three straight games with 11 or more PPR points; Dak Prescott had his first multi-TD game since Week 2 (in Week 7). Minnesota blitzes at 73.9% — no other team is even at 50%; Jared Goff shreds the blitz, with only Lamar Jackson higher in EPA per dropback against the blitz; Goff's passing-yards prop was 259.5, which both hosts called fair and stayed away from. Detroit allows the most fantasy points to opposing QBs. Vikings QB Kyler Murray — zero top-12 QB finishes this year, best QB15 in Week 5 vs. New Orleans, 25th of 35 QBs in EPA per dropback. Broncos RB Jonah Coleman — described as the best Denver RB because he does not tip the offense's hand: good as both a runner and receiver, whereas J.K. Dobbins is a zero as a receiver and RJ Harvey is a zero as a runner. Steelers RB Dylan Sampson — 47% of snaps last week, ran a route on 39.5% of Deshaun Watson's dropbacks, nearly double Quinshon Judkins; Pittsburgh has allowed 285 receiving yards to RBs, third-most in the NFL; hosts like Sampson over 11.5 receiving yards. Falcons WR Drake London vs. Cover 3 — 4.23 yards per route run, highest among 317 WRs/TEs; Tampa runs Cover 3 at the NFL's second-highest rate; hosts like London over 77.5 receiving yards. Falcons QB Michael Penix — no INT since his first start, but 10th-highest turnover-worthy throw rate; worst among 37 qualified QBs in EPA per dropback under pressure; Tampa generates pressure at the league's worst rate and runs man coverage at the fifth-lowest rate, while Penix is much better vs. man. Patriots WR A.J. Brown vs. Miami — Miami deploys Cover 3 and Cover 6 at a 50% clip; Brown averages only 0.59 yards per route run against those coverages. Dolphins QB Malik Willis — QB2 in fantasy points per game over his last two starts, running more.
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Packers — Love is almost a weekly starter; Watson, Golden, Kraft and Jacobs are all easy starts because the target tree is so condensed. Carolina — Bryce Young is a streamer only until he shows otherwise; the only start consideration offered is Week 10 vs. New Orleans (and again Week 14), with Denver next week tough. Eagles — a mess, with sell-off speculation (Dallas Goedert and others named); Mackay Lemon is on the WR3 radar while Smith is out. Buffalo-Baltimore is the early-window game of the week; Lamar back and DJ Moore if back are wanted at full strength; hosts expect a lot of points and took the over. Seattle backfield — expect an ugly rotation for at least a couple of weeks; if Charbonnet and Price are both healthy, Wilson should go to the bench, and a Charbonnet/Price split like last year's Charbonnet/Kenneth Walker split would be navigable. Jets backfield — Allen has earned a larger share, but Hall's receiving ability keeps him ahead of Allen in projections; if you have Hall, play him if active. Denver — Waddle is hard to sit because of draft capital but has been a lineup killer; Sean Payton saying he wants Marvin Mims more touches drew reporter pushback because Payton has said it many times without it happening. Rams backfield — hosts pick Corum over Antonio Williams for a flex spot, despite close projections, because the Rams are 6.5-point favorites and Corum may get enough ground volume without needing routes. A.J. Brown vs. Rashee Rice — lean Brown, with low confidence, because his role is huge, even though the Miami schematic matchup is bad; Brown vs. De'Andre Swift for a flex — Swift is the easy answer. Jets WRs — start both Garrett Wilson and Omar Cooper Jr., bench Ladd McConkey; if DJ Moore does not play, plug McConkey back in; Wilson should get peppered with targets after the sideline scene. Dallas-Arizona — high-scoring, 54.5 total tied for highest on the slate; hamstrings scare the hosts because they linger and can be aggravated, so Pickens at less than 100% opens a WR3 fill-in role for Flournoy; Wilson should be good to go; Dallas starters Lamb, Pickens, Dak and Javonte Williams involve no real decisions; Ferguson has completely disappeared; Flournoy is a flex even if Pickens plays. Minnesota-Detroit — a sneaky shootout candidate; strength-on-strength is Minnesota's blitz vs. Goff's blitz-beating; if you roster Kyler Murray and do not play him against Detroit, hosts ask why he is on your roster — this is the spot to use him, or drop him. Indianapolis-Jacksonville — a fire sale for Indianapolis; Jacksonville -10 at home off a bye; watch the Tootin/Chris Rodriguez backfield, with Rodriguez cutting in more last time and potentially closing if Jacksonville builds a big lead. Las Vegas-Jets — a sneaky shootout; watch both backfields: Hall/Allen on the Jets side, and Mike Washington looking like the better runner than Ashton Jeanty on the Raiders side, while Jeanty's receiving alone would make him WR22 in fantasy points per game. Cleveland-Pittsburgh — total only 40, but the teams put up 51 earlier this year; Warren is loved, Dowdle is earning touches despite being a pain, and Sampson is a prop angle. Atlanta-Tampa — Tampa ran all over Carolina last week and did not need Baker Mayfield; hope for Egbuka back; Penix has back-to-back three-TD games and a safe floor, but the no-INT streak looks fluky given his turnover-worthy rate; London is a prop play. Late window — Chargers-Rams: Los Angeles is coming off a bye and said it would simplify the offense; hosts argue a good coach fits scheme to talent, citing Omarion Hampton at 56.8% success on man concepts vs. 35.3% on zone, and criticize a three-man backfield on a winless team. Kansas City-Denver: Coleman is Denver's guy because he can run and catch without tipping plays. New England-Miami: Willis should keep running; the Patriots' three-man backfield with Corey Kiner means you cannot feel good starting any of them, and Henderson is unusable. Sunday night — both teams may be without their lead RBs; Rashad White is a top-20 RB, while Mitchell as a lead back is hard to trust. Monday night — Chicago-Seattle could be an NFC Championship preview; Monangai is expected to miss again; the Bears trading two first-round picks for Quenton Nelson signals they believe they are a Super Bowl team. Trade question — CeeDee Lamb, Tootin and Isaiah Likely for Bijan Robinson, Tucker Kraft and Omar Cooper Jr. or Keenan Allen: hosts would stay put, calling it lateral and selling low on Tootin, even though Bijan is the best player in the deal.
