SuperFlex — Malik Willis over Aaron Rodgers for rest of season and for upside.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement, including the Stank Squad:** John Hansen's Week 8 "Stank Squad" — high-end players to fade, especially in DFS: QB Matthew Stafford (Chargers allow the seventh-fewest passing yards and 10th-fewest passing TDs; Stafford is at 6.85 yards per attempt with an 85 passer rating vs. Cover 3/4; Puka Nacua sees a 23% target-per-route-run reduction vs. two-high looks, which the Chargers run 57% of the time, eighth-most; expect the Rams to run about 35 times; Hansen also calls this a nice Corum spot); RB Jonathan Taylor (Jacksonville allows the fewest rushing yards and fifth-fewest schedule-adjusted fantasy points to RBs; Taylor ceded 31% of snaps to Seth McGowan last week; Taylor is better on man/gap runs at 4.25 yards per carry and 57% success, but worse vs. zone, while Jacksonville allows only 2.88 yards per carry at 41% success; Jacksonville allows the seventh-most receiving yards to RBs, but Taylor had only three catches for 14 yards total in two games vs. Jacksonville last year); RB Quinshon Judkins (Pittsburgh allows the second-fewest adjusted yards before contact and sixth-fewest rushing yards; Judkins is better on man/gap runs, but Pittsburgh allows only 2.63 yards per carry on those at 29.8% success; Pittsburgh allows the fifth-most receiving yards to RBs, but Sampson led Judkins in route share last week, 40% to 21%, and Judkins' Week 4 receiving line of six for 43 came with Sampson out); WR Carnell Tate (Bengals/Cincinnati defense cited as allowing the second-fewest receiving yards per game, 58, and fifth-fewest receptions per game, five, to outside receivers; DJ Turner is called "the eraser" at 0.15 fantasy points per route run; Tate also has a shoulder injury and a higher-than-usual dud chance despite a good coverage matchup); WR George Pickens (Arizona runs two-high safeties at the highest rate; Pickens' yards per route run more than halves vs. two-high, while Flournoy gets a 17% target-per-route-run boost; Arizona runs Cover 4 or Cover 6 48% of the time, a league high; Flournoy has 0.29 targets per route run vs. Cover 4 over the last six seasons; Arizona is a slot funnel — 13th-fewest schedule-adjusted points to outside WRs but ninth-most to the slot; Ferguson is dismissed); WR Justin Jefferson (failed to top 8.5 PPR points in three of his last four games; Detroit allows the 12th-most receiving yards and eighth-most receptions to outside WRs, and the coverage matchup is good, but Hansen projects shadow coverage from DJ Reed and says defenses double Jefferson because they know the Vikings want to get him the ball, leaving Kyler to check down to T.J. Hockenson in the best TE matchup); TE Sam LaPorta (only a 3% target share and one ball last week after contract talk; Minnesota allows the fewest receptions, fifth-fewest receiving yards and fourth-fewest schedule-adjusted points to TEs; Minnesota runs two-high at the fourth-highest rate, against which LaPorta sees a 20% drop in targets per route run). Elsewhere, Kelly/Dolan say they love Hockenson this week, which is the opposite side of the LaPorta fade. They also say: if DJ Moore plays, he is in; if Hall plays, he is in despite split fear; if Brown plays, play him for role despite the bad schematic matchup; and for Willis vs. Rodgers, Willis is the clear rest-of-season choice.

#### 9. ROTOWIRE FANTASY SPORTS TODAY — "Week 8 Preview" — Oct 30, 1h 37m
Jeff Erickson and Jim Coventry.
- **(a) Injuries/availability:** Christian Watson — hospital comeback, made a late catch, got his yardage prop early, hurt on a deep ball. Carolina — both top corners out, pass rusher Jaelan Phillips down, plus a first-day-of-practice injury to a player rendered "squiring"; Carolina fired its defensive coordinator after the game, per the hosts. Carolina OL — Taylor Moton lost last week, Damien Lewis could not make it to the bell, three OL down; Rashee Walker was a turnstile, rookie Monroe Freeling was thrown in. Bryce Young — sacked six times, zero scramble attempts. Trades — Jeffrey Simmons to Chicago for two firsts and a fourth; Dexter Lawrence to Cincinnati; Quenton Nelson to Seattle; DeForest Buckner traded away by Indianapolis; Sauce Gardner returning to play, as is Tyler Warren (per a cited tweet — flagged as a secondhand report in the transcript). Tampa WR Emeka Egbuka — ruled out, not because of the concussion but because of the foot; Todd Bowles did not characterize it as a foot setback; Egbuka had fully practiced the day before and had cleared concussion protocol, but was ruled out again. Patriots/Eagles items — A.J. Brown not at practice again, hosts do not think he plays; Trayvon Henderson not at practice with an illness, to be played down to the wire; Eagles RB Saquon Barkley at practice, WRs DeVonta Smith and Wicks not at practice. Ravens QB Lamar Jackson — will play, practiced all three days, confirmed during the show; Coventry warns this is a high-ankle sprain and last year's hamstring return brought compensatory foot/toe/ankle injuries, so he is nervous Jackson is not 100% and could suffer a setback. Bills WR DJ Moore — non-contact jersey, sprained AC joint, missed two games, pattern of limited practices then not playing; hosts have trust issues. Bills TE Dalton Kincaid — non-contact jersey with a chest injury. Both Moore and Kincaid were reported as questionable during the show. Titans WR Carnell Tate — did not practice again, status doubtful, may be ruled out. Cardinals RB Jeremiah Love and WR Michael Wilson — both cleared to play; Love missed the last two games and was compromised by an ankle injury; Tyler Allgeier showed up with a toe injury, which hosts speculate could be like Kyler Murray's year-long foot issue last year because the team did not want him playing. Lions — Brian Branch is back, Kirby Joseph might be back. Late notes — Chiefs WR Rashee Rice will play Sunday vs. Denver after a hamstring injury; Eagles RB Saquon Barkley returned to practice after missing the first two practices, with a hamstring and subsequent back compensation injury, likely questionable for Sunday night, making pivots hard; Elijah Mitchell was picked up as a contingency; pivot options named include Treylon Burks (cleared from a foot injury), Chig Okonkwo, Antonio Williams and Washington pass-catchers, plus Bears WR Khalil Raymond as a Monday-night emergency pivot (only 30 yards total in his last four games, but Cole Kmet is out for the season and Chicago cannot go full-time with Sam Roush or three-TE sets). Patriots WR A.J. Brown — knee flared up in Wednesday practice, could not finish, did not practice Thursday or Friday, officially questionable; hosts do not think he plays, but if he is on the field he gets targets in an easy matchup. Vikings RB Aaron Jones — cleared for Sunday vs. Detroit, lowering Jordan Mason's ranking. Also: Kenny Gainwell questionable; Michael Wilson cleared; Love cleared; Moore/Kincaid questionable; Lamar will play; Josh Downs will play; Brian Robinson will not play. Browns OL — TE/RT Titus Howard has a concussion and had not practiced; C Elgton Jenkins also questionable and did not practice, which hosts say flips the Browns-Steelers game because T.J. Watt could line up on that side.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Packers — Jacobs 27 touches for 52 yards, six catches for 13 yards, two TDs, 23 PPR points; hosts call that unsustainable and note Jacobs needs one yard to get two, needs three to get two. Jacobs' career workload — two 300-carry seasons in 2022 and 2024, a knee injury last year, down to 4.0 yards per carry last year. Packers scoring — 27 vs. Carolina, 17 vs. Detroit, 23 vs. Dallas; upcoming New England, Minnesota and the Rams after a bye, when Miles Garrett and Trent McDuffie may be back. Packers OL — a converted guard at third-string center, Laken Tomlinson starting at guard, John Williams at center, Aaron Banks back; hosts say the line was not good even before injuries. Bryce Young completion percentages in his last five games — 54%, 70% vs. Detroit, 51%, 51%, 56% Thursday. Ravens — Derrick Henry scored only 12.9 PPR points combined in his last two games; Buffalo is weak against outside zone, Henry's specialty. Zay Flowers — Declan Doyle is moving him around and isolating him on linebackers; his TD cited was a bubble screen with zero air yards; with Tyler Huntley there was no downfield connection for three weeks; his longest-reception prop moved from about 15.5 to 25.5 at DraftKings after Jackson's return was expected. Flowers ranks — Coventry WR4, Erickson WR5, with Lamb ahead on volume. Lamb vs. Arizona history — last year 85 yards, 2023 53 yards, 2022 51 yards, 2020 64 yards; Dallas is 1-7 vs. Arizona in the cited stretch. Kincaid — 33% of his catches last year went for 20-plus yards; Week 1 five for 130, Week 2 seven for 95, then targets of three, two and two, then eight targets vs. Las Vegas for only seven for 38 — hosts call it a mystery. Bengals RB Chase Brown — longest run this year only 13 yards, third-worst for a starting RB; Samaje Perine has Cincinnati's longest run at 16 yards, from last week vs. Baltimore; Brown still gets a ton of targets, and hosts like his over on rushing plus receiving at 77.5. Titans RB Tony Pollard — hosts fade him; his rushing prop is 66.5 yards, higher than Jonathan Taylor's because of Taylor's matchup, but Cincinnati stops the interior run and Pollard's staff uses him inside, not outside where he would have an advantage; Michael Carter is the receiving back if Tajay Spears is out, and a Bengals lead could game-script Pollard out. Bengals WR Ja'Marr Chase — no 100-yard game this year, career-low 21% target share, 11.0 yards per reception; concussed in the Jacksonville game and terrible in Week 1 vs. Tampa in under-center looks; upcoming schedule is Tennessee, Atlanta, Pittsburgh with no corners, Washington and New Orleans vs. Kool-Aid McKinstry, which hosts call a very strong next month if Burrow turned a corner in the second half last week. Cardinals RB Love — 21 carries and five catches vs. San Francisco before the ankle issue; hosts say Arizona cannot overwork him. Love vs. Jordan Mason — Coventry picks Love because Arizona will throw to him; three catches would put Mason at a disadvantage, while Detroit is decent vs. the run and Mason is probably not a Bijan Robinson-level back who can beat them, plus Minnesota's left guard is out for the season. Vikings QB Kyler Murray — in the cited season: 69% completions, 3,850 yards, 21 TDs, 11 INTs, 572 rushing yards and five rushing TDs; hosts say he has never been a bum/bust except for a toxic five-game stretch last year, but Erickson still ranks him only QB15 this week, behind Bryce Young (which he says he feels shame about) and ahead of Penix and Mahomes. Vikings offense — Murray was knocked out early in Week 1 and missed Week 2; Jefferson was knocked out on the second drive and missed the next game; Addison missed two games; Hockenson was concussed; the first drive last week was the first time everyone was together all year, and the game plan vs. Indianapolis was very conservative. Colts QB Daniel Jones — ruptured Achilles last year on his plant foot, cannot drive the ball, defenses dare him to throw over the top and compress the field, which hurts Josh Downs, who is explosive but has no vacated space. Colts/Jaguars — Trevor Lawrence is surgical in the middle of the field but not to the sidelines; Indianapolis' corners Gardner and Ward are outside corners, not inside players; Parker Washington, Jacoby Myers and Brenton Strange should benefit, plus Tootin and Chris Rodriguez, who looked great in the last game. Jonathan Taylor — Coventry RB6, Erickson outside the top 10; Erickson has Jalen Warren RB5 and Tootin RB4; Warren is playing better than Kyren Williams and close to Cook, with only Walker, Bijan, Gibbs and Taylor overall ahead of him on a play-by-play basis, per Coventry. Raiders RB Ashton Jeanty — broken-tackle rate at the 18th percentile, yards after contact at the 14th percentile, averaging only 2.2 yards after contact, yet 71% of his yards are after contact because he is hit behind the line so often; last three rushing lines: 10 for 27 vs. New England, 10 for 22 vs. Buffalo, eight for 15 vs. the Rams; receiving keeps him in double-digit PPR every week, with catch totals of at least three in two games, four in two games, plus a couple of sixes and an eight. Jets — Omar Cooper Jr. has 12 targets over the last two games, catching 11 for 218 yards; Garrett Wilson had only three targets last week and six the week before, nine total vs. Cooper's 11 in two games — hosts say a market correction must come because Wilson is a superstar who has never been in a good situation. Breece Hall — another limited practice; Erickson notes Hall was hurt in Week 3 and has missed about three to four weeks (transcript inconsistent on the exact count — flagged), and would not be shocked if Hall does not play or is very limited in snap count. Brock Bowers — shut out in the first half vs. Buffalo by Christian Benford, then 11 PPR points in the second half; six for 73 vs. the Rams, the No. 1 defense vs. TEs, is good but below the 18–20 points expected for his draft capital; upcoming San Francisco, Seattle, Denver and Cleveland worry the hosts, who would take McBride over Bowers in a redraft. Browns-Steelers first meeting — DK Metcalf five for 115 on nine targets, Warren 17 for 93 on the ground, no Dowdle in that game, Darnell Washington and another player both caught TDs, Roman Wilson was involved before injury; Judkins was thrown to seven times, with early-down screens to mitigate Pittsburgh's pass rush. Judkins' early film — Baltimore 10 for 61, Green Bay 18 for 94, Detroit 21 for 82 vs. a good run defense, Minnesota 23 for 110, then defenses packed the box once they realized Cleveland could not pass; Cleveland replaced its entire starting OL in the offseason, now passable rather than tragic. Tampa RB Bucky Irving — 31 PPR vs. Dallas, but 6.9 vs. Pittsburgh, 6.1 vs. Green Bay and 7.8 vs. Minnesota; three games under eight points in his last five, plus a 24 and a 31; nine touches on the first drive vs. Carolina as a workhorse, looking like his pre-injury rookie self, but hosts debate boom/bust vs. moderate floor with boom potential. Falcons TE Kyle Pitts — targets in his last four games of three, six, seven and five, catching most of them, with at least 13 PPR points in each of the last three games vs. about three per game in his first four. Chiefs WR Rashee Rice vs. Denver — Denver plays man coverage with Patrick Surtain, and Rice had nine targets, six catches and only 38 yards vs. Denver in the cited Week 11 game last year, after 141 yards vs. zone the week before; Erickson ranks Rice in the mid-20s to 30s for his first game back.
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Green Bay cannot stay one-dimensional, especially with two-deep shells in vogue and a bad OL; the Carolina injuries made Thursday look better than the Packers' recent scoring trend. Baltimore-Buffalo is the best game on the slate; Jackson's return should help Henry and unlock Flowers downfield, and hosts are back to betting Flowers' longest reception over after the line moved. Lamb should be bet on talent despite the Arizona history. Moore/Kincaid are the key Buffalo injury watches; Moore's return would also unlock Kincaid, but the Bills love three-TE sets and running James Cook, so hosts ask why they cannot do both. Cincinnati-Tennessee is a smash spot for Chase Brown with Simmons gone: Tennessee must drop more bodies into coverage against Chase and Higgins, leaving room for Brown, especially as a checkdown option. Fade Pollard for the reasons above. Do not worry about Chase; Higgins is also very good. Arizona-Dallas: Love is a floor play in most leagues because depth does not allow a sit; Bam Knight is a waiver/flex consideration because Love cannot be overworked and Allgeier may not even be active; Michael Wilson is exposed and slow — last year's surge came with Jacoby Brissett playing on fire, trailing every game, and Wilson getting a ton of targets; now Joey Porter Jr. can lock him down and another seven-to-10-point game is likely. Dallas' defense cannot tackle, does not defend the flats, and is not even average despite hopes for Christian Parker; Kenny Clark was called terrible and Rashan Gary a net negative in the cited discussion. Minnesota-Detroit: the Vikings are 6-0 but the offense is ugly, with nothing connecting downfield; Murray got 37 rushing yards last week and needs that component, but he is not a good pocket passer and not great throwing on the run either; Jefferson's production is hurt and Addison is only a break-glass emergency start; Coventry is more optimistic that the offense will click once everyone is healthy together, while Erickson is more cynical, citing McBride, Marvin Harrison Jr. and Wilson all improving once Brissett replaced Murray. Atlanta-Tampa: Penix cannot move or create under pressure, and Todd Bowles sends pressure every week, so Penix needs time to get the ball to London, Bijan Robinson and Pitts, or a screen/after-catch play to bail him out; Tampa's defense is called the best defense on a 2-5 team, playing great defense for four weeks while the offense let them down. Indianapolis-Jacksonville: losing Buckner and Nelson is huge; Jacksonville can run and throw in the middle, and if it builds a double-digit lead it may not throw much, which fantasy managers must note; Taylor is still a start because of volume and receiving work, even if Erickson has him outside the top 10 and will not use him in DFS. Las Vegas-Jets: a 1.5-point spread, not an interesting real-life game but interesting for fantasy; Wilson must get the ball after the Cooper surge, possibly at the expense of TEs and RB targets; Hall's status and Allen's role are central, and Aaron Glenn's love affair with Allen dates to last year, so Allen will play even if Hall returns. Cleveland-Pittsburgh: both teams are 5-2 surprises; Pittsburgh should attack through the air, Cleveland is not a great defense despite Denzel Ward playing at a high level, Warren should be effective, and Watson makes mistakes and takes sacks — the first meeting had a Watson pick, two fumbles (one lost) and only two sacks, which hosts think could change; Cleveland's nice schedule (Tampa flailing, Carolina without Coker, Pittsburgh, Jets, Ravens with Huntley, Tennessee) should be discounted. Atlanta-Tampa and Cleveland-Pittsburgh are covered as lower-offense games. Final rapid notes: Rice is a start-if-you-have-him but a terrible matchup vs. Denver man coverage; Barkley should be put in the flex, not RB, if possible, to preserve late-game pivot options; A.J. Brown is a game-time decision with an easy matchup if he plays, but Drake Maye is hitting TEs and RBs a ton and not WRs much.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Erickson and Coventry agree on most big calls — Flowers is WR4/WR5, Chase will rebound, Taylor is still a start despite the matchup, and Green Bay's run game is not trustworthy. They disagree in degree on Murray: Coventry defends Murray as an accurate, average-or-better NFL QB who was in a toxic situation last year, while Erickson is more cynical and still ranks him only QB15. They disagree on Taylor vs. Warren: Coventry keeps Taylor RB6 on volume and big-play ability, Erickson has Warren ahead of Taylor this week. They are both nervous about Jackson's high-ankle return, both worried about Bowers' upcoming schedule and draft-capital return, and both skeptical that Jeanty's rushing will improve without better blocking and a head of steam. They are confident in the Flowers breakout under Declan Doyle, in Tootin and Warren as high-usage backs, and in a market correction toward Wilson in the Jets passing game.

**Cross-show conflicts and ambiguities flagged by the capture (Friday batch A):**
- **Emeka Egbuka:** Fantasy Alarm says already ruled out; Ratcliffe says limited in concussion protocol and expected a full Friday practice; RotoWire says ruled out for the foot, not the concussion, after clearing protocol and fully practicing the day before. Do not treat any single show's status as final. (The X digest in Part 5 independently flags Egbuka's status as disputed in-corpus; the official designation governs.)
- **Saquon Barkley / DeVonta Smith (Eagles):** Fantasy Football Morning says neither practiced yet this week and doubts either suits up; RotoWire says Barkley returned to practice (hamstring plus back compensation injury) while Smith/Wicks did not practice. Timing within Friday may explain part of the difference.
- **Breece Hall:** Fantasy Alarm says trending/expected to play; Ratcliffe says limited back-to-back and still uncertain after missing last week; Fantasy Football Morning ranks him RB17 assuming he plays but fears a near 50/50 split; RotoWire says another limited practice and would not be shocked if he does not play or is very limited.
- **DJ Moore (Bills):** All shows agree on limited/non-contact/shoulder concern, but differ on optimism — Fantasy Football Morning says if he plays he is in, Ratcliffe says 50/50 without a full practice, RotoWire stresses trust issues from the prior limited-then-out pattern, and Coventry expects him to play because 21 days off and a non-contact jersey are precautionary.
- **Jeremiah Love / Tyler Allgeier / Bam Knight:** Name spellings vary across transcripts. All shows agree Love missed two games with an ankle issue, was limited, and is a floor/flex play with workload-management risk; RotoWire adds the Allgeier toe speculation.
- Several other names are auto-transcript garbles and should be verified before use: "Omar Cooper Jr." (Jets WR), "Mackay Lemon" (Eagles WR), "Carnell Tate" (Titans WR), "Jeremy Bernard" (Steelers WR, as rendered by Ratcliffe), "Kyle Monangai/Rohan" (Bears RB), "Tin Man" (Patriots WR in Ratcliffe), "Kobe Bassett" for Jacoby Brissett in Fantasy Alarm, and "Kante Turin" for KaVontae Turpin in Fantasy Football Morning. The shows' stated teams, matchups and numbers were preserved rather than substituted with outside roster knowledge.
- No episode failed: no transcript was unavailable and no playback failed. RotoWire's "More episodes" list also showed a separate Oct 31 "Week 8 Preview" — the Oct 30 episode at the assigned URL is the one captured here.

Berry on Judkins/Sampson and on Isaiah Likely (team context garbled in the show; 16% target share with J.J. McCarthy vs. 30% with Jameis Winston, only four targets last week): nervous about Likely, but still an every-week starter who should be featured more after the bye. On Patrick Mahomes vs. Denver: closer to his 11.7 floor than his 18.0 ceiling, QB2 territory; Denver was the fifth-hardest QB matchup and had not allowed multiple QB touchdowns in its last three games, with Patrick Surtain possibly returning. On Tony Pollard, Titans, without Spears/Tate: a bigger game is possible on volume, but Cincinnati was a poor matchup — fourth-fewest fantasy points allowed to RBs, the league's best run-stop rate vs. RBs, only four RBs over 10 fantasy points all season. On Washington's receivers with Terry McLaurin out for the season: Antonio Williams was closer to his 12.0 ceiling than his 5.1 floor, with an expanding role, vs. a Philadelphia defense allowing the second-most fantasy points to WRs and 10 WRs scoring 14+ against it. On a Brock Bowers-for-Ashton Jeanty trade: do not do it; Jeanty was RB13/RB12 in PPR points per game, at least 10 points in every game, but with atrocious rushing efficiency and passing-game salvation, possibly injured, with a Week 13 bye; Bowers had at least eight targets in every game and 20–28-point upside weeks, with only a Fernando Mendoza QB-change concern. On Dallas–Arizona: Cupps had Dak Prescott QB2 (QB1 on DVP-adjusted projections at 24.1 points; 23.8 in prediction markets, behind only Josh Allen), with a 275.5 passing-yard line, the highest on the slate, in the slate's highest-total game at 54.5 points. On Michael Wilson: Cupps was higher than consensus (WR17 vs. consensus WR19; Valenzuela WR18), arguing his slot/wide, motion and target-depth role was unchanged without Harrison Jr. and recent weeks were variance. On Khalil Shakir, Bills, vs. Baltimore (24th in DVP vs. WR): a mid/high-end WR3 in PPR, above his WR44 consensus, with market ranks around WR29–WR31. On Caleb Williams, Bears: Cupps QB14 personally, but DVP-adjusted QB21; Seattle was second vs. QB and allowed about seven fewer points on average to QBs. On Sam LaPorta, Lions, vs. Minnesota: still a top-three TE for Cupps (market TE3 behind Bowers and Trey McBride) despite one catch for 18 yards last week — a bounce-back call. On Jared Goff vs. Kyler Murray: Valenzuela ranked Goff higher even vs. Minnesota and would keep Shakir/Malik Washington rather than drop one for Murray. On "Omar Cooper" vs. Jameson Williams vs. Concepcion (names as transcribed): hosts leaned Cooper, with Concepcion close; Jameson Williams was boom-or-bust, with a Minnesota blitz slant-home-run path but a very low floor, and prior-year lines vs. Minnesota of 4 for 66 and a touchdown, and 2 for 37. On Ryan Flournoy, Cowboys, vs. Arizona (19% rostered): a deeper-format option, possibly about four catches for 60 yards. Survivor note: do not take Dallas; Cincinnati was expected to be the most popular Survivor pick, Dallas 7-point favorites (tied third with New England), Jacksonville 10-point favorites.

Cupps was unusually confident in Dak (start over anyone except Josh Allen) and in Wilson's role, while warning Burden and Caleb Williams were matchup traps. - **(c) Main Sunday takes:** The central debate was Jalen Coker vs. Luther Burden from Thursday night. Raph started Coker because Seattle was by far the stingiest team vs. fantasy WRs while Green Bay allowed almost twice as many WR points per week, and because RotoBaller's rankings (Nick Mariano) had Coker a few spots ahead. Hayes said it was not close: Burden was the top target-getter/centerpiece of Chicago's offense with Caleb Williams, while Coker was only WR2 on Carolina with a fading Bryce Young; Burden should be started regardless of matchup, and preseason rankings/draft capital proved the gap. Raph conceded the lesson ("start your studs," no tinkering), while noting Coker had won weeks and that McMillan vs. Coker was closer to 1/1A when healthy (season touchdowns cited as McMillan two, Coker three). Hayes countered that Coker was definitely not the best WR on his own team and that Burden should "dog-walk" Coker by season's end; Burden needed only 10 half-PPR points to beat Coker's Thursday total. On Seattle's backfield: sit all of Charbonnet, Price and Emmanuel Wilson for Week 8 — Charbonnet's first game back would have a ramp-up, Wilson could fall from first to third in the pecking order, and Chicago's trade for Jeffrey Simmons (described as possibly the NFL's best defensive tackle) further cooled the matchup. On A.J. Brown: if healthy, play him — in 10/12-team leagues, waiver WRs were speculative and none should be ranked over him as a stream, and a 1–6/2–5 team might as well "go down with the ship." On the Jets: Omar Cooper Jr. was a great talent/find, but Garrett Wilson had only four targets in multiple weeks and was answering trade questions; outside Cooper, no Jet was trustworthy weekly, and Mason Taylor would be started by many this week but likely regretted. On Michael Penix Jr.: a matchup/bi-week streamer only, not an every-week starter, despite two strong games — Atlanta remained run-first through Bijan Robinson, and Tampa Bay's defense had looked professional the last two weeks (though probably not as tough as Chicago/San Francisco). On Jonah Coleman: if forced to pick one Broncos RB for Week 8, pick Coleman, but overall do not want to start any Broncos RB vs. Kansas City's good front seven; hosts took the under on 8.5 fantasy points for Coleman and reserved him for 14+ team leagues or deeper 12-team leagues with an extra flex. On Michael Wilson vs. Dallas: the problem was Jacoby Brissett, not Wilson — Brissett was inaccurate, throwing many downfield balls out of bounds — but the Cowboys were far softer than Denver's secondary (even without Patrick Surtain), so Wilson remained a dice roll until a QB change. Kendrick Bourne was in the same dice-roll category. On T.J. Hockenson: a serviceable streamer/pinch-hit with a floor when available, not a comeback-player-of-the-year season. DFS — RotoBaller optimizer ("Rod") DraftKings optimal lineup, pulled before a joked-about data-center outage: Dak Prescott $6,300 vs. Arizona; Ja'Marr Chase $7,700 vs. Tennessee ("turnstile"); Jahmyr Gibbs $9,500 vs. Minnesota (banged-up risk noted); Bhayshul Tuten $5,900 vs. Indianapolis (hosts said he could score 20 points); Bucky Irving $6,400 vs. Atlanta (trust him; Baker Mayfield helps; a rollercoaster year); Omar Cooper $4,800 vs. Las Vegas (described as a lot underpriced — a rookie with no long track record, a known DraftKings pricing gap); Colby Parkinson $3,100 (check the rib/questionable status); Kendrick Bourne $3,900. Trade upgrades cited: DeForest Buckner was now a 49er (formerly the Colts' top run-stopping defensive tackle) and Jeffrey Simmons was now a Bear (formerly Tennessee's best defender), so hosts doubled down on attacking Indianapolis and Tennessee fronts, i.e., the Jaguars and Bengals matchups.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** The show's sharpest disagreement was Coker vs. Burden (matchup/rankings vs. talent/role). Hayes was confident in Burden as an every-week start, in Puka playing through the calf issue, and in fading all Seattle RBs in Week 8. Raph was more cautious on Golden's drops (eight on the season, with a risk of losing snaps if other Packers WRs were healthy, and a talented WR whose name is transcribed as "Avion Williams" noted as not having the same drop issue), on Penix as only a streamer, and on Coleman as only a deep-league play.

- A.J. Brown: all four shows present him in a New England/Miami context with a knee issue; Fantasy Life and Footballers were the most negative (do not play even if active), while RotoBaller said a healthy Brown should still be played over waiver options.

Christian Watson suffered a chest injury on a 50-plus-yard reception Thursday night, went to hospital in Green Bay, and returned; a host described it as a sternoclavicular-joint issue where a blood vessel underneath can be at risk if dislocated. Packers kicker Trace Mack — transcript "Trace Mac" — missed an extra point against Detroit and again Thursday night and was expected by the hosts to be cut. For Chicago, a player described as missing the last few games with turf toe — name garbled in the transcript as "Common" — was on a bike for the first time at Wednesday practice, was not expected to play Monday night, had a better chance the following Sunday night, and could be held through the Week 10 bye; DeAndre Swift and Rohan Johnson were the comfortable alternatives. Cole Kmet was noted as hurt. Jalen Waddle's chest injury was aggravated falling on the ball on a late reverse call while Denver led by 14; he had missed/limited practice days, was not headed to IR in the hosts' understanding, but chest injuries can linger. Pat Bryant was on IR after a strong camp and was expected to serve the minimum four weeks before returning. Bo Nix was recovering from ankle surgeries, with a mental aspect to the recovery acknowledged, before a 62-yard untouched touchdown run. Denver's Pat Surtain II — transcript "Patzer" — had missed games, with Denver 6-0 in the last six games he missed.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Mahomes averaged 28 points per game over the first four weeks, including 38.8 against Indianapolis. Love's Arizona backfield: Tyler Allgeier had been terrible, Bam Knight had looked good, but a host expected Love plus Allgeier as complementary pieces, with Knight reduced to a handful of touches or possibly inactive when Love returns. Justin Jefferson was WR30 with only two double-digit games. Denver: Jonah Coleman immediately led the backfield in snaps, routes, touches and scrimmage yards after returning from IR with no ramp-up; RJ Harvey was the preferred receiving/change-of-pace back; J.K. Dobbins was touchdown-dependent at the goal line; Coleman and Harvey could both produce RB2 numbers. In the Thursday game, Chuba Hubbard had 81 rushing yards and a touchdown plus seven receptions for 38 yards; Josh Jacobs had 21 carries for 39 yards and a touchdown plus six receptions for 13 yards and a touchdown — 27 touches at 1.9 yards per touch; Jordan Love threw for 288 yards, three touchdowns and one interception; Bryce Young threw for 209 yards, one touchdown and one interception on 23-of-41; Tetairoa McMillan had six receptions for 57 yards on 11 targets; Jalen Coker had two receptions for 37 yards and a touchdown on five targets; Christian Watson had eight receptions for 113 yards on 10 targets in the recap (seven receptions were mentioned earlier in the show — flagged discrepancy) plus a fumble; Tucker Kraft caught all six targets for a touchdown. In FAB, Elijah Mitchell was the top pickup in 94 primetime leagues with a high bid of $207; Devin Singletary was picked up in 75 leagues at $277; Jonah Coleman had the week's highest bid at $611; Mackay Lemon was $376; Caleb Douglas was $375; Mason Taylor was picked up in 50 leagues at $108. Alvin Kamara had double-digit scoring in three of four games, almost all receiving. Ollie Gordon had four straight games of 14-plus points since replacing the injured Devon Achane in Miami. Omar Cooper Jr. had back-to-back 100-yard games and led the league in yards per route run in a small sample of two games plus one Week 1 route. Zay Flowers was described as top-10 without Lamar Jackson and top-five with him. Ryan Flournoy played in Dallas' 11-personnel sets on 73% of snaps, third-highest in the league (league high 78%).
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Mahomes was QB14 for the hosts this week against Denver — still start him unless you have Goff, Jordan Love, Matthew Stafford or Jayden Daniels. Love was a buy-low in trade leagues and a hold-and-hope in NFFC. Jefferson was reframed as a boom-bust WR2, still a start, with a good matchup against Detroit, but with low confidence. Chicago at Seattle, Monday night: guest Zach Pearson predicted Seattle 26-23, citing home field, while saying a Bears win would be a statement game; containing Jaxon Smith-Njigba (about 15 yards per reception, only one game under 100 yards) would require Jalen Johnson, rookie Malik Muhammad and help over the top, plus blitzing from Tyrique Stevenson — transcript "Tyler Gordon," who had four sacks in two games — to disrupt Sam Darnold. Denver vs. Kansas City: guest Arne Stapleton predicted Denver 24-17, citing Vance Joseph's defense and Bo Nix taking another step; the blueprint was Seattle's, making Mahomes beat Denver while containing Kenneth Walker, who had 173 yards in the first meeting, 147 after first contact, with about two dozen missed tackles. The hosts' Six Pack: Kamara is a low-end RB2/high-end RB3 on receiving work; Ollie Gordon is locked in as an RB2; the Rams should go closer to a 50/50 Kyren Williams/Blake Corum split, with Corum the home-run X-factor (two touchdowns called back vs. Las Vegas, including a 42-yard run on a phantom penalty); Omar Cooper Jr. is a plug-and-play WR2 (WR23 this week); Zay Flowers is matchup- and quarterback-proof; Ryan Flournoy is a WR3/WR4 streamer against Arizona in a high-scoring game as Jake Ferguson falls out of favor. Waiver/defense calls: Mitchell is a one-week rental, not a long-term answer, because Saquon Barkley, Tank Bigsby and others will return; Singletary is a desperation play who somehow finishes with about 700 rushing yards most years, with the Giants possibly trading for Tony Pollard or adding Gus Edwards; Mackay Lemon could become a steady WR2 in the second half with Tanner McKee's pocket passing suiting his slot/YAC game; Caleb Douglas has the highest ceiling in Miami despite volatility, over Malik Washington (highest target share but low average depth of target) and Chris Bell; Mason Taylor is a talent stash in case a Garrett Wilson trade opens targets; for defenses, Washington over Tampa Bay was preferred because of expected turnovers from a depleted Philadelphia team.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** Dave Kluge was confident Mahomes rights himself; Tom Kessen was more cautious on Wentz, saying Wentz can work for a couple of games before turning back into Carson Wentz, while Kluge thought Wentz could take the Vikings job. On Jonathan Taylor, Kluge did not expect a trade unless a team offered a conditional third that could become a second, because Indianapolis was firm on a first-round pick and Taylor had nearly 2,000 career touches and was past the age curve; the McCaffrey-type return was not expected. On Brian Thomas Jr., Tom Kessen said last year he could have fetched a first, now a fourth-round pick, conditional third, is fair, and Washington made the most long-term sense (pairing with Antonio Williams for Jayden Daniels, plus their college connection), while Kluge favored the Rams, especially with Davante Adams out, and named Indianapolis as a dark horse if Alec Pierce's long-term availability is a concern. On the Simmons trade, the Bears guest called it a win-win and not an overpay, given late-first-round hit rates and the lack of good rookie defensive tackles.

#### 17. FOOTBALL DIEHARDS — "Week 8 Lineup Calls & Trade Talk" — Oct 29, 1h 37m — FULL TRANSCRIPT CAPTURED
- **(a) Injuries/availability:** Matthew Golden was officially questionable Thursday night with a shoulder injury; hosts warned of end-game aggravation, citing DJ Moore as a reminder that one bad fall can aggravate such an injury, but called Golden a top-15 wide receiver and said the host himself was playing him. Ke'Shawn Nixon, a top-scoring IDP corner, was downgraded to questionable on game day. Ja'Marr Chase was said to be okay despite an ankle issue and was back at practice. Amon-Ra St. Brown and Jahmyr Gibbs were back at practice for Detroit. Saquon Barkley, DeVonta Smith and a Philadelphia player transcript-rendered as "Davian Wick" were out/not practicing. Damien Pierce headed to IR; Jayden Blue is the backup but Elijah Mitchell, reverted to the practice squad, was expected to be elevated and most likely to play the lead role, best used only as a Barkley handcuff on Sunday night. A.J. Brown was listed with an ankle issue plus a newly flared knee issue, with Sunday status up in the air. Caleb Williams was coming back from a hamstring injury; Ben Johnson said the staff did not want to take the "magic" out of his play, while seeing him play more on time and on rhythm. Lamar Jackson was trending in the right direction, with Jesse Minter expressing optimism but noting it depended on response through the week. DJ Moore was discussed for a Buffalo return after missing games; Joe Brady noted how tough the absence had been. Carnell Tate — transcript "carne Tate" — did not practice for Tennessee. Emeka Egbuka was in a regular, not non-contact, jersey in Tampa Bay while trying to clear concussion protocol. Kenny Gainwell was back at practice for Tampa Bay. Aaron Jones had "some confidence" for Sunday for Minnesota, with doubt remaining. Bryce Young had been hampered by a bad knee for three weeks but was a full participant at practice. Taylor Moton was out for the season with a broken fibula after battling blood clots. Rashawn Walker — transcript "Rashid Walker" — yielded seven pressures and three sacks last week. TreVeyon Henderson missed practice with an illness. RJ Harvey was present for only a portion of Thursday's Denver practice, with no word on why. Chris Godwin had a veteran rest day. Brian Robinson missed practice for Atlanta. Tyler Warren had a second limited practice and was trending toward returning after two weeks out. Breece Hall had a second limited practice, Braelon Allen was up to full practice. Brian Thomas Jr.'s hands were bothering him, and Adonai Mitchell was not on the Jets injury report.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Chase Brown had gotten the touches but not the rushing production; Samaje Perine usage had increased, and the Simmons trade left a vacated spot in Tennessee's defensive line. With Lamar Jackson, Baltimore scored 116 points in four games (29 per game); with Tyler Huntley, 53 points in three games (about 17 per game). Derrick Henry had single-digit production in each of the last two games despite good volume. Jacoby Lane ran 28 routes and ranked second on Baltimore in targets last week. Rome Odunze had seven catches for 108 yards total in his two games back, described as a 3-4 catch, 50-60 yard player with touchdown upside but several drops. For Carolina, the Panthers were the NFL's No. 2 passing offense at just over 276 yards per game. Green Bay's defense allowed under three yards per carry. Jordan Love had completed over 60% of his passes only twice this year, was pressured about as much as any quarterback, and threw deep as much as anyone. Footballguys projections cited on the show: Golden started the weekend as WR11, fell to WR28, then rose back to WR14, just ahead of Jalen Coker at WR15; Lamar Jackson was QB4, Jordan Love QB8, Caleb Williams QB9, Jacoby Brissett QB10, Jayden Daniels QB7 at a projected 20 points vs. Caleb Williams at 18.8 against Seattle. T.J. Hockenson was the preferred tight end over Dallas Goedert because no team was more generous to opposing tight ends in catches, fantasy points, yardage and touchdowns than Detroit. Kenny Gainwell had 88 rushing yards in a favorable game even in a three-way split with Bucky Irving and Sean Tucker.
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Play Golden if active; if out, pivot away from a double-Panthers stack to Colston Loveland. Jalen Coker was called an easy pivot off Golden in a vacuum, but the hosts would not play McMillan and Coker together. Play Jacobs and DeAndre Swift as the running back/flex answers for one caller, with Jordan Mason as a consideration only if Aaron Jones is ruled out; Breece Hall was the top choice if he plays, with Mason next if Jones is out, then Swift. Jayden Daniels was preferred over Caleb Williams, largely on respect for Seattle's defense, though the hosts said Williams is close to matchup-proof and would not talk a caller out of him. Take the Jeanty/Mike Washington/Denzel Boston side over Travis Etienne, Emanuel Wilson and Luther Burden, with Etienne expected to be in a multi-way split in New Orleans despite his price tag. Take Mike Evans for Chase Brown — "hit submit" — but consider timing so as not to give an opponent a free week of Brown, especially with the Simmons boost. Give up Bhayshul Tuten to get Golden rather than giving up Jalen Warren for George Pickens, and do not trade Bijan Robinson for Puka Nacua; Golden's role was seen as more consistently high-end than Pickens'. Take Bijan Robinson plus Jacoby Lane for Kyren Williams plus Malik Nabers as a fair deal, though Lane was not a player the hosts were confident starting this season. Reject Ladd McConkey plus a piece for Antonio Williams plus Chig Okonkwo in a regular league, because no Chargers wide receiver was projected inside starting territory (better than WR30-40). Play Trevor Lawrence over Caleb Williams against Indianapolis, where Lawrence has his best numbers against any opponent. Play Lamar Jackson over Jordan Love if you have a contingency quarterback, because Love's ideal scenario is run-heavy with big plays. Play Javonte Williams and Parker Washington as flex choices over Golden and Pickens for one caller. Play Rashee Rice over Denzel Boston if Rice returns. Play Ollie Gordon over Tyler Warren at flex. Play Gainwell over Tyler Allgeier for a flex spot. Jeff Bell's segment: Coker is an easy Golden pivot; Jacobs is a top-15 running back despite the short week (70% of snaps last week, with an 80%-plus bell-cow role possible if that was a ramp-up); Hubbard should still be treated as a bell cow; for Philadelphia, Mitchell is the desperation play over Blue given the long list of backs favored over Blue, though Bell also noted Blue would be favored if active and Mitchell keeps a special-teams role; Chase Brown was RB14, an opportunistic back who benefits when Cincinnati leans into its passing identity; if Love returns in Arizona, Love is the only comfortable play and even that is not comfortable, with no right answer between Allgeier and Bam Knight; Chig Okonkwo gets the biggest bump from Terry McLaurin being out for the season, over Antonio Williams and Raylan Burks, because John Bates plays inline and Diggs' role was said to be unchanged; Zach Charbonnet should be deferred to a solid Sunday option over a risky Monday-night wait against Chicago's Simmons-upgraded line, unless desperate; Brock Bowers should only be traded for a top-12 player, with Mendoza expected to still scheme him heavily (70 yards in both "down" weeks, a 1,200-yard pace); Dalton Kincaid is a live-with-the-downside upside play unless you have a locked-in top-five tight end, and DJ Moore's return could change defensive attention and blocking-tight-end usage; take "Conception" over Denzel Boston for rest of season, because Concepcion's volume plus added explosives is more likely than Boston adding short/intermediate volume; Omar Cooper Jr. is legitimate, but Adonai Mitchell's return plus multiple tight ends is the concern; dynasty buy Mackay Lemon after his Monday-night touchdown flash.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** The hosts said Golden's shoulder does not look like a major, multi-week issue, but advised waiting until inactives were announced before trading for him so as not to give an opponent Tuten for a week Golden might miss. On the Simmons deal, Tennessee GM Mike Borgonzi was quoted as saying it took two first-round picks plus a fourth (with a fifth coming back, effectively upgrading a fifth to a fourth) to force the deal, after about 50 calls. On Jonathan Taylor, Shane Steichen said Taylor had not requested a trade and Taylor confirmed it, saying he was proud to wear the horseshoe, but a local reporter was cited as saying it would be malpractice for Indianapolis to hold him during a multi-year rebuild; the hosts advised having fallback plans for a surprise Sunday deactivation. On the Packers, Bill Huber predicted Green Bay, citing Carolina's bad run defense, Jalen Phillips as Carolina's best pass rusher, and two starting Carolina cornerbacks and two starting offensive tackles being out; he also expected Green Bay to be a buyer at the deadline, though not for a blockbuster, since the team had not bought at a deadline since 2010 and lacked a first-round pick. On Carolina, Jeff Hawkins predicted Bryce Young would "take care of business" Thursday night, while the Diehards hosts leaned Green Bay at home.

#### 18. ROTOBALLER RADIO — "Week 8 Breakdown and Thursday Night Football Preview" — Oct 29, 49m — FULL TRANSCRIPT CAPTURED
- **(a) Injuries/availability:** Indianapolis began a fire sale; Josh Downs requested a trade, Jonathan Taylor was the big fish, and Keenan Allen was a potential wait. Tyler Warren practiced on a limited basis Wednesday. Saquon Barkley did not practice Wednesday and was now dealing with a back injury; Damien Pierce went to IR. DeVonta Smith was limited Wednesday. Detroit: Jahmyr Gibbs missed practice with a shoulder injury and Amon-Ra St. Brown missed with an ankle injury; Aaron Jones also did not practice for Minnesota. Lamar Jackson practiced in full and was back for Baltimore. DJ Moore returned to practice after a shoulder injury, a failed attempt to play through it, and a couple of missed games; re-injury risk was stressed. Matthew Golden was listed questionable for Thursday night with what the hosts described as a shoulder/ankle combination issue. Travis Etienne was discussed as a waiver claim with a grade-two hamstring issue in one caller's context, while another host said Devon Achane was not coming back this season — flagged as conflicting with other shows' "Achane back after the bye" framing. Jordan Tyson's return was discussed as a threat to Devon Vele's value.
- **(b) Usage/backfield/target-share:** Seth McGowan was a stash candidate in deeper leagues if Jonathan Taylor is traded, because a trade could open major volume. Elijah Mitchell's history was recapped: 963 yards, five touchdowns, 4.7 yards per carry as a 2021 rookie with San Francisco; five games in 2022; 11 games in 2023; no games in 2024 due to injury; one game and one target in 2025 with Kansas City; three carries for 11 yards last week for Philadelphia — the hosts said he had touched the ball only three times since 2023 before that Philadelphia game, a claim that sits awkwardly against the 2025 target noted in the same segment. Washington allowed 100 rushing yards per game. Minnesota allowed only 155 passing yards per game and about 98 rushing yards per game; Detroit allowed the most passing yards per game at almost 292. Kyler Murray had not thrown for 250 yards yet this season (season high 247, three touchdowns, two interceptions) and had only 132 rushing yards. Bryce Young finished inside QB10 in six games this year — QB3, QB7, QB6 and QB8 in four of them, QB22 and QB28 in the two bad games against Cleveland and Tampa Bay. Green Bay allowed almost 26 points per game to running backs and 10 total touchdowns to running backs (nine rushing, one receiving). Carolina allowed 153 rushing yards per game, 29 points per game to running backs and nine rushing touchdowns. Josh Jacobs had 11 carries vs. six for Marshawn Lloyd last week, but seven targets, second on Green Bay, catching five for 55 yards plus a passing touchdown — over 16 PPR points on receiving work alone. Jalen Coker had target counts of nine, nine and 13 in normal games, excluding an injury-shortened Week 3, a Week 5 bye and a Week 7 blowout. Geno Smith's weekly quarterback finishes were cited as 28, 16, 3, 30, 25, 19 and then 5 last week. RotoBaller consensus rankings cited: Tucker Kraft TE3, Brenton Strange TE12, Jared Goff QB12, Geno Smith QB19.
- **(c) Main Sunday takes:** Do not bench Jonathan Taylor, but be cautious playing other Colts at Jacksonville, a good defense, especially with Downs' trade request hanging over his value. If Barkley is out, Mitchell would start Sunday night against Washington and was a waiver priority, with a double-digit-point opportunity for either Barkley or Mitchell; Jaden Blue was the next desperation step. If Gibbs and Amon-Ra are activated, start them despite Minnesota — they are stars and bye-week alternatives are thin — but temper expectations and add upside elsewhere if your lineup is otherwise full of 8-10 point churners. Do not trust Kyler Murray as a stream even against Detroit's terrible pass defense; the hosts put him on "fantasy suspension until further notice" and preferred Jacoby Brissett as a stream in the Cardinals-Cowboys game (54.5-point total). Start DJ Moore if he makes it through the week, but keep Keon Coleman as insurance; one host said he would play both Moore and Coleman in a 14-team league. For Thursday night: Chuba Hubbard and Tetairoa McMillan cannot come out of lineups; Bryce Young is an acceptable stream, with Green Bay allowing 208 passing yards per game and Carolina's pass-defense yardage number (196 per game) called misleading because Tampa Bay threw only 11 times while running for about 250 yards; Josh Jacobs is a "smash spot" with no hesitation; Christian Watson, Golden if he plays, Tucker Kraft and Jordan Love are all in lineups. If Golden is out, Savion Williams or Bo Melton were mentioned only as desperation pivots. Bryce Young was preferred over Baker Mayfield, despite Atlanta's defensive pop-ups, because Green Bay was seen as more vulnerable than Atlanta coming off a big performance. Call answers: Tucker Kraft over Brenton Strange at flex (Kraft TE3 vs. Strange TE12; Carolina blowout potential was a tiebreaker); Jared Goff as the safer 13-15 point play over Geno Smith, whose range was five to 25 points — take Geno only if behind and needing upside, with Max Crosby motivation noted; Bryce Young over Kyler Murray; accept Devon Vele plus Ollie Gordon for Michael Wilson ("auto accept") because Gordon is rolling and Vele had been as good as or better than Wilson some weeks; play Jalen Coker Thursday night rather than waiting on DeVonta Smith for Sunday night, given Smith's McKee/connection and re-injury uncertainty plus Mackay Lemon's emergence; trade or claim Travis Etienne over holding a player who needs a McCaffrey injury to gain value, with Etienne expected to be the stunt/starting back after the bye.
- **(d) Confidence/disagreement:** The hosts disagreed with the idea that Carolina could be trusted after being run out of the building twice, with Dave Canales' preparation questioned, yet still backed Bryce Young's season-long profile (top-12 quarterback) and a bounce-back. On Goff vs. Geno, one host leaned Goff for safety and ceiling if Detroit's offense rolls, while the other framed Geno as the gamble play. On the Thursday score, Anthony Aniano predicted Packers 30-27 (total 57, over 47.5, Packers not covering 3.5), while Josh Hayes predicted Packers 33-28 (Packers covering by five). Both took Jordan Love over 259.5 passing yards (averaging about 280) and Bryce Young over 252.5 passing yards.

**Cross-show notes (Thursday remainder):** "Jeremiah Love" is used across shows for the Arizona rookie running back; "Omar/Omari Cooper Jr.", "Jeremy Bernard," "Conception," "Carne/Carnell Tate," "Behe/Bhayshul Tootin," "Trace Mac/Mack," and several caller-team attributions are transcript renderings and are flagged above rather than silently corrected. The Simmons trade price is consistently two first-round picks plus a fourth, with Diehards adding that a fifth-round pick came back to Tennessee. The most consistent Week 8 injury thread across the captured shows is Golden's Thursday-night shoulder risk, Waddle's chest injury, Barkley/Pierce thinning Philadelphia's backfield to Mitchell/Blue, DJ Moore's return, and the Detroit/Minnesota practice absences for Gibbs, Amon-Ra St. Brown and Aaron Jones.

# PART 1 — GSE ANALYTICS / ADVANCED-METRICS SWEEP (Oct 31, 09:08 CDT run)

Window: posts after status ID 2116170306087759894 (9:08 AM CDT Thu Oct 30) through 9:08 AM CDT Sat Oct 31. Metrics-only sweep (22 named accounts incl. both @NFLResearcher / @NFL_Researcher variants + Following feed + 5 searches: EPA, CPOE, TPRR, pass rush win rate, aggressiveness). Three lanes: A (9:09–9:46 AM CDT; viewed 150, in-window 96, analytics 48, non-analytics 48, dup 0), B (9:09–9:34; in-window 40, analytics 25, non-analytics 15, dup 0), C (9:09–9:27; feed 2 passes ~53 + ~45 viewed, unique captures 14). Final watermark for the next run: status ID 2116532392425099604.

## NEW metric definitions (the run's headline output)

### SCRACK — @PattonAnalytics (Steven Patton, Data Scientist @StatRankings)
- **Definition (as printed in the post, status 2116264185013137658): SCRACK = SCRAMBLE + SACK.** Precisely: EPA summed over exactly two play types — sacks and scrambles — as a single QB pocket-behavior number ("Net Production on Sacks and Scrambles," 2026).
- Credited proponent: @schwartzsteins ("a major proponent" per Patton's own Oct 30 post).
- Values as reported (chart 2116234920402317622, PARTIAL — bar EPA values not numerically printed): **only Jayden Daniels and Josh Allen have positive SCRACK.** Thread replies (not counted in profile totals): Jaxson Dart −0.83 SCRACK EPA; Drew Lock +0.07, the only other positive. Worked sensitivity: Bo Nix's 60+ yard TD scramble was worth 5.48 EPA; his chart SCRACK sat at **−12.25 (14th of 35 qualifying QBs)** and would fall to **−17.73 (22nd)** counting/without that play (the thread's framing: one explosive scramble moves Nix ~8 rank positions and does not rescue a season of sack/scramble leakage).
- Engine read (innovation-kernel note, attributed): a two-event EPA composite; both components are pbp-available (sacks exact; scrambles charted in nflverse as QB runs on pass plays — approximate), so an in-house cousin is computable with the scramble half labeled a proxy. Layer: matchups (QB pocket behavior vs pressure scheme).
- The X For You PM run independently captured the chart's axis-key annotation (see W8 in Part 3): "Left number is sacks, right number is scrambles" — the reading key that makes the underlying chart usable.

### TOXIC — @PattonAnalytics (chart definition; footer Data: @nflreadr)
- **Definition (as printed on the chart, status 2116244495339782497): TOXIC = explosive plays minus turnovers, as a rate** — a difference-of-counts composite, plotted against EPA per Play (PARTIAL scatter; per-team values not transcribed). Layer: everything-else / efficiency context.

### Down-conditioned EPA trackers — @BrainyBallers (2027 NFL Draft, COLLEGE prospect data, labeled as such in the posts — not current NFL players)
- **QB EPA per play on passing downs**, Week 9 (2116532392425099604): Darian Mensah +0.58 top; full top 10 captured; CSV `brainyballers-2027-draft-qb-epa-passing-downs-wk9.csv`.
- **TE EPA per play on 3rd down** (2116532140733247495): Green +1.60 top; rows 1–7 legible, 8–10 PARTIAL; CSV filed.
- **RB EPA per play on 3rd down** (2116531637790142875): Baugh +0.92 top; rows 1–5 legible, 6–10 PARTIAL; CSV filed.
- Construction: EPA per play situationally filtered (passing downs for QBs; 3rd down only for RB/TE) as the ranking axis for college prospect classes — EPA conditioned on down/situation rather than overall. Footer: account branding only, source not stated.

## AM watchlist dossiers (W1–W14)

### W1 — @The_Coach_A — Rams weak-side stunts: "phone booth" run-fit (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/The_Coach_A/status/2116519972625096906 (Oct 31, 08:17 CDT). Source: @The_Coach_A (MatchQuarters); link resolved+read: MatchQuarters Substack, "Mike Macdonald's Super Bowl LX Defense: All-22 Masterclass" (2026-02-13).
- WHAT: A run-fit mechanism: (1) overload the formation's strong/front side so the RB's designed path is closed; (2) stunt/pressure the weak side (away from the TE) so the cutback runs into a constricted space — a "phone booth." Two named executions: a TAM stunt (3-technique slanting to the A-gap, keeping the Mike LB clean, SS filling the runway) and a weak-shifted Nose with a Will B-gap insert ("B-gap stab"). Post applies the family to Rams vs Raiders; the linked article is the Macdonald/Seattle case: "passive pressure," ~25% blitz rate and ~25% stunt rate (3rd in NFL per the article), doctrine "Stop the run, have some fun." The two-dial language (blitz rate vs stunt rate) describes defensive aggression without collapsing both into "pressure."
- USE/LAYER: Matchups layer (defensive front structure vs run scheme). Needs charted stunt rate/direction (not in nflverse pbp); honest in-house proxies = defensive stuffed rate and explosive rate allowed. Pairs with @sfdata9ers' Stuffed vs Explosive Runs table.
- RISKS: Two-clip film claim (Rams application UNVERIFIED beyond the post); the article is about Seattle, not the Rams — do not cite it as Rams evidence; stunt figures are the article's own (SumerSports cited inside it).
- TEST: Rams'/Seattle's defensive stuffed/explosive-allowed splits from nflverse vs the mechanism's predicted direction. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY; the blitz-dial vs stunt-dial vocabulary ADOPT.**

### W2 — @The_Coach_A — Flores' tree: Zimmer base, Fangio-consultant adjustments (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/The_Coach_A/status/2116546605067485205 (Oct 31, 10:03 CDT). No external link; author's expert claim.
- WHAT: Coaching-tree attribution for Brian Flores: base system = Mike Zimmer tree; modern layer = adjustments from Vic Fangio's consultant year with him; conclusion — the defense is sui generis and tree labels mislead. Usable content: a defense is better predicted by its own logged tendencies than by its coordinator's tree label. (Thread context: "the Eagles tried to hire 'Fangio guys,' which didn't work.")
- USE/LAYER: Matchups layer (coach vs coach). Corroborates GSE's tau/coach design (coaches as individuals, not tree priors); preview Minnesota from Flores' own blitz/mug/stunt tendencies, never a generic "Fangio defense."
- RISKS: Single-author, unsourced; "consultant year" detail UNVERIFIED against team records. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY.**

### W3 — @Timo_NFL — Endgame simplification: tiny WP edges are still edges (FOOTBALL / method)
- Thread (5 posts, Oct 31, 07:31–08:23 GMT): https://x.com/Timo_NFL/status/2116432753050767674 · /2116435260564992425 · /2116435649024704598 · /2116445623423553769 · /2116445924276756537. Source: Timo Riske (@Timo_NFL, SumerSports ecosystem).
- WHAT (method argument, five parts): (1) Judge endgame decisions on the SIGN of the WP change, not its size — moving WP from ~0 to +0.2% is an infinite relative gain and the correct call even when the absolute number looks trivial (the thread's example: going on 4th & 25, down 4, own 30, 20 seconds left). (2) The "small edge" in late-game aggression was derived by logical deduction first — enumerate what MUST happen for a comeback, price each branch. (3) Benjamin Morris did the full empirical version at FiveThirtyEight 10+ years ago (poster citation; the poster himself flags the piece may no longer be accessible; UNVERIFIED by direct fetch). (4) WP-model outputs carry real uncertainty — which is why a hand-solvable simplification (a closed-form bound solvable "in 60 seconds by hand" that survives model error) is valuable. (5) The two articles under discussion add little beyond Morris; the referent articles are NOT linked in the captured posts (gap).
- USE/LAYER: Situations layer (endgame state) feeding coach/4th-down reasoning (GSE's tau_hat / TauFitter wp_gap machinery). Two operating rules proposed: (a) in endgame reasoning, report the sign and the must-happen chain, not just the WP delta — a +0.2% that is the only non-zero path outranks a +2% among several paths; (b) keep a hand-checkable bound beside every model WP number GSE prints.
- RISKS: Argument, not measurement; the 0.2% figure is the poster's paraphrase of an unlinked article; Morris attribution single-source.
- TEST: Last 20 settled endgame 4th-down spots in GSE's warehouse — model wp_gap vs must-happen chain side by side, count sign disagreements. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY as a post; the two operating rules are ADOPT candidates for the reasoning style guide.**

## AM feed dossiers (addendum, A1–A12 — browser feed pass 1)

### A1 — @corbin_young21 — First-read target share + FPOE buy-lows (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/corbin_young21/status/2116477784935837979. Source data: @FantasyPtsData charting.
- WHAT: A usage-efficiency screen: top 24 WR/TEs by **first-read target share** (share of team dropbacks where the QB's first read is that receiver; min 50 routes, 25% route share, Weeks 1–7), cross-read against **FPOE/G** (fantasy points over expectation per game). Buy-low rule: high first-read share + negative FPOE/G = designed usage the box score hasn't paid yet. Named cases: DeVonta Smith −1.48, Chris Olave −1.1, Malik Nabers −3.03 FPOE/G. Chart columns: TPRR, FPRR, YPRR, aDOT, 1D/RR, FP/G, xFP/G.
- USE/LAYER: Usage layer (first in the weekly order). First-read share is charted/tracking-class (a judgment of QB read order) — not derivable from pbp; FPOE/xFP are reproducible in structure from nflverse opportunity data (targets, air yards, red-zone looks) with a stated expectation model. First-read share is a QB-trust stat — a leading indicator the week before a receiver's line moves.
- RISKS: Charter variance; 50-route minimum admits small samples; FPOE sign flips fast. TEST: rebuild the xFP/FPOE half from nflverse W1–7 and check the three named buy-lows' ordering survives. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (screen; first-read share as cited input).

### A5 — @_avichawla — Continuous batching / LLM output nondeterminism (TOOL / method)
- Post: https://x.com/_avichawla/status/2116505679691407710. WHAT: Under continuous batching (vLLM-class schedulers), a request shares GPU batches with strangers' requests and batch composition can change the numerics of its own output — same prompt, different logits; the piece covers token budget, KV-cache allocation, preemption. SYSTEM: his model-routing stack — reproducibility hygiene: outputs from shared/batched endpoints (free tiers especially) are not bit-stable across load, so model-comparison evals must fix batching conditions or average over runs. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY.**

The poster's caption demands an unnamed receiver (not named in captured text; not guessed) be thrown the ball. RISKS: the chart plots teams, blurring player-level reading; both stacks proprietary. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (validation pattern cataloged).

### A7 — @cmikesspinmove — Yards per cover snap, underneath defenders (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/cmikesspinmove/status/2116232577141104813. Charting source not named in captured text (CITED).

### W1 — @sfdata9ers — The luck factor: opponent FG misses (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/sfdata9ers/status/2116649916659347600 (Oct 31, 16:54 CDT). Full table in image, UNREADABLE via x_search beyond the text lines.
- WHAT: A luck accounting metric: for each team, the expected points its opponents failed to score because their FG attempts missed — kick distance–based make probability summed over opponent misses, credited to the defending team as "luck." Text endpoints: **1. PIT +13.5 EP, 2. LAC +13.1, 3. DET +11.9 … 31. KC & NO 0** (no opponent-miss luck at all). It is a regression ledger: points a defense "allowed" that it did nothing to prevent from being attempted.
- SOURCE: @sfdata9ers; no vendor named in text; construction is standard (FG make probability by distance is a public, stable curve; pbp carries every attempt).
- USE/LAYER: Everything-else / luck adjustment feeding matchups and situations. Fully computable in-house from nflverse pbp + an in-house distance-based FG probability curve. Scale: +13 EP over 7 weeks ≈ ~1.9 points/game of defensive flattery. Zero-luck teams (KC, NO) are the mirror signal — their defensive numbers need no discount.
- RISKS: Poster curve unstated (values CITED); some "misses" are defense-caused (blocks, pressure on the kick operation); ~20 opponent attempts/team is a small sample.
- TEST: Recompute from pbp with an in-house distance curve; check the PIT/LAC/DET ordering survives. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (rebuild candidate — high value; PM synthesis test #1).

### W3 — @sfdata9ers — FantasyPtsData chart pair, run game: RYOE + Missed Tackles Forced (FOOTBALL)
- Posts: https://x.com/sfdata9ers/status/2116641611874701531 (RYOE, Weeks 1–7, Data: @FantasyPtsData) · https://x.com/sfdata9ers/status/2116628273795088798 (Missed Tackles Forced, min 70 carries, Data: @FantasyPtsData). Chart values in images, UNREADABLE via this route.
- WHAT: Two complementary RB run-game measures. **RYOE (Rushing Yards over Expectation):** actual rushing yards minus model-expected yards given the carry context (defenders in box, down/distance, blocking surface) — the public cousin of the corpus's RYOE/xRY work. **Missed Tackles Forced:** charted broken tackles under a 70-carry qualifier — the creation stat that separates back skill from line skill. Together they decompose a rushing line into context, elusiveness, and outcome.
- USE/LAYER: Usage layer (RB evaluation) feeding DFS/props. GSE can approximate RYOE from pbp covariates; MTF is charted-class (PFR's free BrkTkl is the in-house substitute, already used for the Oct 29 missed-tackle reproduction). RYOE-minus-MTF disagreement is the trade screen: high MTF + low RYOE = creator on a bad offense (buy-low; the Taylor archetype); high RYOE + low MTF = scheme product.
- RISKS: Image-locked values; expectation model proprietary; MTF charting varies by vendor. (Same-day companion: @KyleM_FF's RB Rushing Efficiency chart W1–8 with YPC/EPA/Success/RYOE/MTF/YBCO columns, PM pass 2 — player rows illegible, LEARN-ONLY.) **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY.**

### W5 — @The_Coach_A — The "Magic" path: Detroit's 3-man interior weave (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/The_Coach_A/status/2116666534735249588 (Oct 31, 18:00 CDT). Link resolved+read: MatchQuarters Substack, Cody Alexander, "Anatomy of a Pressure: The 'Magic' Path" (Aug 29, 2025, Paid; public portion read).
- WHAT: A named pressure path — "Magic" — defined mechanically: from a five-man front, the three interior linemen weave (boundary DT + Nose stunt toward the field; the field DT wraps back to the boundary) while both edges contain, opening a runway for the wrapping DT through the vacated interior. Detroit application: a base 4-3 that dresses up like a 3-4, running the weave vs shotgun play-action under Campbell. The article's mechanism: the 5-0 front forces offenses into predictable man-blocking schemes, and the 3-man interior stunt creates a "pick" and a free rushing lane.
- USE/LAYER: Matchups layer (pressure design vs pass protection). Engine content: a 5-man front forces man-blocking rules, making interior weaves a known-answer problem for the defense. "Dresses up like a 3-4" is a front-identification warning for personnel-based matchup stats.
- RISKS: Single-clip film claim (Detroit application UNVERIFIED beyond the post); full article paid; stunt direction/frequency is charted-class. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (mechanism reference verified).

### W6 — @The_Coach_A — The "Wrap" stunt out of a double-mug (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/The_Coach_A/status/2116621233760452630 (Oct 31, 15:00 CDT). Link resolved+read: MatchQuarters, "The Raiders Boss (Load) Wrap Stunt" (Apr 4, 2024, Paid; public portion read) — the Wrap family's reference (Boss/Load overload front isolating Maxx Crosby vs the 2023 Colts). Post = Bengals-vs-Ravens application.
- WHAT: A protection-breaking mechanism, step by step: the double-mug (both LBs walked into the A-gaps) forces the protection to declare — RB takes one mugger, Center takes the other — then both declared answers are wrong: the Center's LB drops into coverage; the DT stunts into the A-gap drawing the Center's vision; opposite, the other LB "boxes" (outside shoulder) the RB's block while the other DT works through the B-gap to "pick" the LT and occupy the LG; that creates a direct lane to the QB for the wrapping Edge; the opposite Edge runs a "fishhook" to force the QB to step up into it. A designed free-runner, not a won rep.
- USE/LAYER: Matchups layer. Mug-look frequency + free-runner rate is the pressure stat that survives box-score blindness; GSE cannot compute mug looks from pbp (cited scheme context), but the reasoning template ("the protection declared, both answers were wrong") is adoptable preview language. The double-mug is also Flores' signature family — W6 is the anti-Flores protection chapter from the offense's side. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY.**

### W7 — @The_Coach_A — Patrick Graham's Nickel run pressures: the cross-dog vise (FOOTBALL)
- Post: https://x.com/The_Coach_A/status/2116575933322637434 (Oct 31, 12:00 CDT). Link RESOLVED + READ IN FULL: MatchQuarters, "Patrick Graham's Nickel Pressures vs. Early-Down Runs" (published 2026-10-30; public portion read in full). The article confirms the attribution (Graham is Pittsburgh's DC).
- WHAT: A run-pressure system for light boxes: in Nickel (5 DBs), Pittsburgh sends interior five-man pressure paths behind Fire Zone (3-under/3-deep) rotations; the signature fit is the cross-dog (Mike "Cross") — one LB attacks the "V" of the Center's neck and cuts off the RB's designed track while the other LB wraps into the opposite gap, putting the RB in an immediate vise as the second/third levels squeeze. Claim: timed run-down pressure is how a Nickel defense survives heavy zone rushing without adding box bodies. "Pressure changes the math; execution kills the play."
- Sourced figures (article's, PFF/FTN data, CITED from the article — NOT reproduced in-house): PIT uses Big Nickel on <5% of plays; Nickel run pressure 18% of calls (20th); **early-down (1st/2nd) Nickel Run Blitz Rate 37% (4th most aggressive); league-lowest −.441 EPA/play on early-down Nickel calls;** Stuff Rate 23.5% (5th); Run DVOA 2nd (FTN); ADOTK 2.3 (2nd). League-wide: run blitzes lowered EPA/run by −.125 and raised TFL+no-gain rate by >7% (PFF, 2025 season).
- USE/LAYER: Matchups layer — the quantified half of AM W1's phone-booth dossier. Preview use: cite the sourced rate (37%) and effect (−.441 EPA/play on those calls), labeled article-CITED. The −.441 figure covers early-down Nickel calls ONLY — never quote it as Pittsburgh's overall defensive EPA.
- TEST: PIT defensive EPA/run split by down (1st/2nd vs later) from nflverse; check the early-down strength direction matches. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (system + sourced rates cataloged).

### FD1 — @dgrifka / @nfl_simulator — Deserve To Win: the method's live track record (FOOTBALL)
- Posts: https://x.com/dgrifka/status/2116696907895263328 + the @nfl_simulator DTW explainer served in the same feed pass (no status ID captured — inventoried, not indexed).
- WHAT: **Deserve-to-Win (DTW):** a post-game deservedness measure built from post-game win expectancy (PGWE — the probability a team wins given the game's full play-by-play profile), distinct from both live win probability and EPA totals; "not EPA, not win probability" per the explainer. The update post applies it live: Baltimore's win at Miami grades as undeserved — "Ravens win in Miami despite not deserving to — 2nd win when outplayed all year (Giants)" — i.e., the metric runs a season ledger of stolen wins (the count is the poster's, CITED).
- SOURCE: @dgrifka / @nfl_simulator; full repo dossier already in the corpus (`~/workspace/x-for-you-sweep/reports/2026-10-30-NFL-SIMULATOR-DOSSIER.md`; github.com/dgrifka/nfl_simulator).
- USE/LAYER: Everything-else layer feeding situations: a stolen-win ledger is a regression screen (undeserved-win teams are over-priced by their record; deserved-loss teams are the buy-low side). GSE can compute a PGWE cousin from its own WP model. RISKS: PGWE depends on WP-model calibration (AM W3's uncertainty file applies). TEST: the repo dossier's standing test (recompute PGWE on settled games, compare the ledger). **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY.**

### FD2 — @karpathy — autoresearch: the autonomous experiment loop (TOOL)
- Post: https://x.com/karpathy/status/2116695689122103490. VERIFIED at the primary source (full README read): https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch — public, MIT, 98,965 stars, 13,647 forks, 36 commits, created 2026-03-06, no releases.
- WHAT: An autonomous research harness: a coding agent edits ONE file (train.py), trains for a fixed 5-minute budget, checks the single metric (val_bpb — validation bits per byte, lower is better), keeps or discards the change, and repeats — ~12 experiments/hour, ~100 overnight. The human never edits Python; the human edits program.md, the Markdown file that programs the "research org." Three files that matter: prepare.py (fixed; data + tokenizer + evaluation), train.py (agent-edited), program.md (human-edited). Requirements: single NVIDIA GPU (tested on H100), Python 3.10+, uv; quickstart `uv sync` → `uv run prepare.py` → `uv run train.py`, then point a coding agent at the repo with program.md as instructions and permissions disabled. Notable forks: macOS (miolini/autoresearch-macos, trevin-creator/autoresearch-mlx), Windows (jsegov/autoresearch-win-rtx), AMD (andyluo7/autoresearch).
- SYSTEM: Agent fleet infrastructure + GSE research lane — as a PATTERN, not GPU code (no H100 in Garrett's lane; Kaggle GPU lane is nearest compute). Transferable design: (1) one mutable file, everything else frozen; (2) fixed time budget makes experiments comparable; (3) one scalar metric with keep/discard discipline; (4) the human programs the org in Markdown. The shape a GSE hyperparameter/weight-search harness should take (e.g., overnight runs over the 2,462 locked games with a frozen scorer).
- RISKS: NVIDIA GPU required upstream (forks relax with quality caveats); results platform-relative by design (README: runs not comparable across hardware); agent editing training code with permissions disabled is sandbox-only. TEST: paper pilot — a program.md for one bounded GSE question (e.g., conformal interval width vs coverage on locked games) with a frozen scorer, 10 agent experiments, no GPU. **Verdict: TEST** (pattern); repo **LEARN-ONLY** for the training code.

### B1 — @WalrusQuant — sports-leader-mcp: a free MCP server for live sports data (TOOL)
- Post: https://x.com/WalrusQuant/status/2116541247326441768 (v0.4.0: 4 new tools — rankings, power index, team stats, futures; fixes to leaders, win probabilities, standings, scoreboards; a v0.5.0 self-reply already exists). VERIFIED at the primary source (README read in full): https://github.com/WalrusQuant/sports-leader-mcp — public, MIT, 6 stars, 2 forks, 37 commits, created 2026-04-12, actively developed.
- WHAT: An MCP server wrapping ESPN's public data surface: **38 tools + 4 resources + 5 guided prompts** — live scores, standings, rosters, player/team stats, per-book betting odds (normalized to implied and no-vig probabilities), full play-by-play, win-probability timelines, injuries, transactions, news, college rankings, BPI/FPI power index, and futures. Plus a historical export: nightly Parquet files (MLB/NBA/NFL/NHL, seasons from 2021 — schedule, box scores, player logs, rosters, injury/transaction history, depth charts, weather, published ratings) queried through the same tools via DuckDB. Recorded discrepancy: the repo subtitle claims "17 sports and 139 leagues," while the README body documents a curated catalog of **30 verified leagues in 11 sports** — the verified catalog is the smaller number; other ESPN slugs "may work" unverified.
- HOW: Node.js 20+; `git clone` → `npm install` → `npm run build` → register as a stdio server (`node /path/dist/index.js`). **No ESPN auth or API keys**; stdio mode has no server-side request quota (HTTP mode adds configurable per-IP limits). Historical data: public manifest on GitHub Releases (companion repo WalrusQuant/bayesratings-data), published nightly ~7:15 AM CT, sha-checked local cache, 36-hour freshness flag via `get_data_status`.
- SYSTEM/LAYER: Agent fleet infrastructure (fleet MCP bus) + GSE data layer. `get_slate` (one call per game day: games, consensus no-vig probabilities, key Out/Doubtful injuries) is a ready-made morning-slate input; `get_injuries`/`get_team_injuries` feed the health layer from a second source; `get_game_probabilities` gives a public WP timeline to sanity-check GSE's own WP outputs (AM W3's uncertainty file is the reason); the Parquet export is a free settled-history source complementing nflverse. Its SKILL.md-in-repo pattern pairs with FD2's program.md lesson.
- RISKS: Tiny project (6 stars, 3 contributors) riding ESPN's undocumented endpoints — breakage risk real; odds quality is ESPN's, not a sharp book's; the inflated subtitle count is recorded; the historical export is a single maintainer's nightly pipeline.
- TEST: Clone, build, call `get_slate` for Sunday's NFL slate and diff its Out/Doubtful list and no-vig consensus against numbers GSE already holds. **Verdict: TEST** (the PM run's highest-priority test — free, MIT, no key, directly on-stack).

### B2 — @lvwerra — DeepSeek dsh-superagent prototype (TOOL)
