### 1:36:19 ALEX'S LEVERAGE LANE — CHRIS RODRIGUEZ
- Alex: "I talked a lot about this slate about how… I agree with the field that the upside is in the wide receivers, not the running backs this week. So, how do I… use that to my advantage when the field is already doing that? Well, I can try to find a way to do that even more extremely than what the field is doing. We have Jonah Coleman. We also have some other really kind of gross cheap running backs. One of them is going to be my leverage."
- Alex on the mechanism: "if the running back position as a whole doesn't have those like separating performances. Think back to… week two… there were like multiple receivers who had 35 plus and maybe there was one running back who had more than like 25. And… when you have a slate like that, I could get eight points from this play and he could be not optimal, but… I could still go get takedowns if I have the right combination of expensive wide receivers who go off and things of that nature. The play is Chris Rodriguez."
- Alex: "Now, Chris Rodriguez is not going to project well. There's like there's no way around that. But he did have a red zone touch on three separate drives in their last game. It was a 34-31 loss, so it was actually a close game. He had 11 carries, 55 yards, looked really good running the ball against a very good Houston defense. And he just didn't happen to score. And it wasn't just like three random touches inside the red zone. It was like he was the guy in the red zone until they weren't able to get, you know, they weren't able to punch the ball in. [Another back] came in and they threw a pass to [him] and he got in. I think [he] had two touchdowns that game and had a third called back. Uh those easily could have been Rodriguez touchdowns instead. He was getting like almost a 50/50 split. It was working well. I expect a lot of the same and I expect the Jags to really move the ball effectively against Indianapolis. It looks like there might be rain in that game. So it might be like a run-heavy game as well in which case they're going to want to mix him in more. So I think he has a very clear path to touches. I think he has a much better path to red zone… touches and touchdown equity than what people realize."
- Alex on the leverage: "the huge thing is if we're going to see ownership on Trevor Lawrence and Parker Washington and certainly a ton of ownership on [Tuten] regardless of what the weather looks like, it's a massive leverage piece if Rodriguez can vulture touchdowns from them." He calls it "very comical to think about, but it's also very… easy to envision a scenario where Rodriguez scores twice and everyone you see on Twitter is like screaming about how… Vegas is rigged and they're getting Rodriguez touchdown… solely because they know everyone has touchdown bets on behalf [Tuten] instead." He sees "a very real path to, let's say, 15 to 20 DraftKings points for Rodriguez where he leverages some of the important chalk on the slate and gives you access to combinations of stud wide receivers with crazy good ceilings. Throw Bowers in there as well. You can get access to Bowers really easy. like you could go Bowers and three stud wide receivers very easily in some builds that start like Jonah Coleman and Chris Rodriguez, something like that. And so I really love this one."
- Alex compares it to Blake Corum: "I think it's reminiscent of the Corum play that we've played very successfully over the last few weeks and I think Rodriguez can be like basically an identical play to Corum except even cheaper and lower ownership and more direct leverage off of some of the biggest chalk on the slate."
- Tambo asks what week they first talked heavily about Corum. Alex: playing Corum on "the… week five showdown slate against Buffalo. I think when we started to talk about him… heavily for DFS purposes was week six against Arizona where he finished with… 9 [carries for] 42 and… two for 18 and a receiving touchdown and he had most of that in the first half and then it was a much closer game than… we all expected and so he really didn't get much run in the second half otherwise it could have been huge and then last week… you finally get the game scripts we were hoping for Corum he get 17.1 with a 42 yard touchdown called back and a five yard touchdown called back. So like 17.1 that… was actually him running bad somehow in that game. That could have been a 30-pointer from him at… I remember he was 4% owned in the afternoon only slate and that was a three gamer. He must have been 1% on the main slate."
- Tambo: "everything you said for C-Rod remind me of it and I found it." He points to the free show page at Ship It Nation: "you go to shows on the page is completely free. Go to shipnation go to latest shows just type in leverage here and then go to week seven or sorry week six leverage lane." The Week 6 discussion was about "projections versus conviction," and viewers can click around 8:14 in that episode for "when conviction conflicts with projections."
- Tambo on pairing the two Leverage Lanes: "I mentioned earlier like Jags D. What if you do that, right? What if that's the payoff difference of him going down? Like if everyone has [Tuten] and a cheap D, you could now have Jags and Rodriguez. It's… I know it seems like it's nothing, but it could be something. You don't need to do that. Maybe the thousand is now T. Higgins up to Chase instead or where people can't get to that or you get Puka and not the Chargers D or there's so many ways that you can use it to your advantage."
- Tambo on Rodriguez's prop: "His rush yards when I saw he was still at almost like 35 rush yards for his prop… I get it, but I'm saying it's like usually in a scenario like this, you see it at nothing. I think Corum was like 20 that week or something. So, you're not really seeing much at all. And then you see… almost 40 for his [rushing + receiving] and all these other things. You're starting to think like, oh, there actually could be something there. So, I do think it's a little bit interesting when we find things like that. I liked your call out quite a bit. You could pair them together, a payup D and the Jags D and Alex's… leverage lane with Rodriguez and then mix some other pieces around it."
- Alex's closing context on Rodriguez vs. Coleman: "Jonah Coleman's rush prop is 44 and a half and Rodriguez is 31 and a half. So… [they] are 13 rushing yards [apart]. Now there's… more to it of course, but… I would argue that Rodriguez might have as much or more touchdown equity than… Coleman given the matchups. So, like, are 13 rushing yards worth 20 percentage points? Like, nobody's using Chris Rodriguez. Maybe this show will boost him up to like one and a half percent instead of 0.1% that he otherwise would have been, but he's going to be insanely insanely low owned for sure."
- Tambo: "Yeah, I like that, man. You can… fit a lot in with… moves like that. So, think about that when you guys are building your lineups out there."
- Closing plugs: Alex — blickanalytics.com, "Use promo code leverage. Use promo code TAMBA," for "roster construction stuff, conditional ownership, contest sim stuff." Tambo — shipnation.com, bookmark the free show page, check "the tidbit that… Alex dropped back in leverage lane episode 6… around the 7 and a half minute mark," and find him on X. ## 9. Production build-break findings — root cause + staged fix

sports-web's last READY production deploy was Oct 29 23:36 UTC (sha 409b86527);
six Oct 30 deploys were CANCELED and the PR #1224 merge deploy (Oct 31 02:18 UTC)
ERRORED with `import_error`. Motif-verified root cause: PR #1224 removed the only
call site of `LIVE_SLATE_COACH_WRITEUPS` in `apps/web/app/packages/page.tsx` but
left the import (line 7); ESLint runs inside `next build`
(`no-unused-vars` = error, `ignoreDuringBuilds: false`), so the build fails at
the lint stage. Fix staged and remote-verified: branch
`fix/prod-build-unused-import`, commit 209babe98, exactly one line removed;
`ignoreDuringBuilds` deliberately NOT flipped. Merge widget sent 07:33 CT;
Garrett never tapped it in chat. **Note — a later entry (flush addendum 6) records
a second, conflicting diagnosis** from the hourly deploy check: production builds
failing on the `next/font/google` fetch of Big Shoulders, with the import fix
"not addressing the font root cause." Both diagnoses are in the log; the
discriminating test on record is redeploying 409b86527 unchanged (READY ⇒ the
import; ERROR ⇒ environment/gate). Artifact: `~/workspace/build-break-2026-10-31/FINDINGS.md`.
